A novel · 84,111 words · English

The Empress, a Watch and Two Thieves Who Happened to Be Passing

An Accidental Theft at Valencia’s Mercado Central

Saturday’s golden light penetrates through the dome, sunbathing the stalls of Valencia’s Mercado Central with a special charm. Each of the market’s streets smells of local products, the international’s exoticism mingles with the smell of meat, salted fish, flowers, tiger nuts, chocolate and baklava, aromas that blend with Valencian, Castilian, English, Japanese and French phonemes. “Què te pose, xiqueta?” (What would you like, love?) is what is most often heard and seems to be understood by everyone.

Foreigners are discovered by their hats, cameras in hand, their socks and sandals, and their purchases of turrón (nougat) in the middle of summer while the locals enjoy the horchata. The restaurants are crowded, red wine and beer with tapas of paella, tortilla and ham. It is aperitivo hour. People crowd into the aisles to enjoy the different flavours.

The squawking of parakeets that have crept into the building can be heard from every corner, it is obligatory to look up to search for them, encountering the scrolls, the coloured light through the stained glass windows representing orange trees and “lo rat penat” (the winged rat/the bat).

A young man in the crowd, with an ungainly air, walks with bated breath, restless, looking from one side to the other, searching for his companion. He moves quickly towards the end of the aisle as if someone were after him. “Aah!”, a startled gasp, a hand squeezes his gently, he turns and observes, it is a girl, at a gesture from the girl, both hurry to leave the corridor, behind them someone seems to advance and stares at them.

― Girl: Two behind us, one in front. We sneak through the tapas bar, run towards the centre and get lost in the crowd. There are drones on top of us -she squeezes the boy’s hand again-. Run! -he exclaims in a whisper.

Without letting go of her hand, the boy runs after the girl until the crowd separates them. The officers run after them. “CHAVAL!!! STOP, IT IS THE CIVIL GUARD!!!”, shout two officers who run after the boy. “Move, lady! GET OFF!”, the boy tries to reach for his friend’s hand.

The officer’s moves and them shouts make the people get rowdy, crowded in the corridors and in the central square. There must be more than 100 people against each other, frightened. The boy quickly slips through the crowd, a rough hand brushes him close, the fingers manage to grab his T-shirt just as he gets lost in the multitude, the force with which the officer crashes into them causes him to fall but still he won’t let go of the shirt and when he pulls the garment a girl is pulled out, no resemblance to the boy he was chasing.

― Officer: Sorry -he says dryly, surprised. He communicates through his earpiece. I’ve lost him. Where is he?! -he snorts in a fit of anger and pushes through the crowd, which is moving to make room.

― Captain: They are there, look for them!

The officers look around at the visitors of the market. They can’t find them.

― Officer: We cannot identify them, sir.

― Captain: They’re here! -he says frustrated, biting his lips. Ask every vendor in the compound to open chambers, lockers, EVERYTHING! -a few seconds to consider the next move-. Order these people out. They will exit one at a time through the north gate, I want each individual stopped for visual identification, glasses and hats removed, whatever it takes.

One of the officers, through a megaphone, announces: “Ladies and gentlemen, we apologise, the market is being closed, please follow the orders of the officers to leave the premises in an orderly manner through the north gate”, and the same instruction is given in Spanish.

The crowd, still frightened, obeys the instructions of the Guardia Civil, and one by one they leave, stopping at the door to be identified. After forty minutes the market is cleared, the gates are closed and a thorough inspection of the stalls begins.

― Captain: -he looks at the videos over and over again- I don’t understand it, the moment they mingle with the group they disappear, it’s not possible… -he mumbles between his teeth. An officer approaches.

― Officer: We can’t find them, Captain.

― Captain: FUCK! They’re playing us again… How the fuck do they do it?! … Here, take the video to IT, let them analyse it together with the camera recordings. It’s impossible for pickpockets to wear prosthetics and change clothes in a matter of seconds… They’re doing something to disappear. It’s unbelievable! -he snaps, on edge, walking towards the door-. We’re leaving -he says into the comms.

After the departure of the security forces, access is reopened. In barely an hour the market returns to normal. The event is commented on in the networks and even a reporter from the local Valencian television is present.

Ω

― Boy: They’re watching us, Nikoleta, you’ve seen it, haven’t you? They know us, I hadn’t even taken his wallet and they were already after us.

― Nikoleta: Lázaro, take it easy -he puts his hands in the pockets of his sweatshirt while he looks from side to side with a nonchalant shrug-. There are more places in Valencia to steal from.

― Lázaro: What are you saying, girl! We are on their radar, look at the racket they set up to catch us -he pinches the fabric of her sweatshirt and pulls it down until Nikoleta stops.

― Nik: Don’t think about it, okay? -she picks up her pace and lets go of the boy’s grip-. It’ll be fine, that’s how it always is. I’m calm, so are you.

― Láz: Like… mmm… reassure me? -he shakes his head in disbelief as she puts her hand on his chest and stops him abruptly.

― Nik: They haven’t caught us, slow down - the difference in height now more noticeable allows Nikoleta to impose herself on him- …It’s all right - Lázaro looks down, looks at his friend’s hand, exhales the air that until now, without knowing it, he had been holding in his lungs.

They are in a small alley with a dirty field and a couple of abandoned cars, between the buttresses that support the façade of the adjoining building. Lázaro takes off a pair of shirts and a pair of trousers fastened with Velcro, removing them in a single movement. The girl removes her wig, earrings and glasses, and pulls her long skirt up into a mini-skirt. They leave the things in a suitcase that was hidden in the open field, it is full of clothes and other tools that they use in their feats to confuse the police. Lázaro throws it over his shoulder and they both start going up the stairs of the Palacio de la Seda and between narrow streets they enter the Carmen neighbourhood.

They live in a narrow old building, in an attic run by an old lady who can’t leave the house, she doesn’t know what these young people do, but it’s not important, they help her get up, go to bed, bring her food, keep the house tidy, do the laundry and although there is more dust than she would like, the company pays well for having them there. They pay a small fee for the shared use of the top floor room, a double room with a small toilet and two small beds, overlooking a square that has been rebuild and at first glance seems far from poverty, but the corners are littered with cardboards, blankets and objects from the homeless who roam the area.

They are both leaning out on the narrow, geranium-filled balcony. He is leaning against the railing, she is leaning against the wall. BBBBBBBRRRRRRRRRRRRMMMMMMMMMM!!!!! A very loud bang shakes the whole city. The shock is followed by a terrible sensation, Lázaro falls to the ground with a tight frown, Nikoleta clutches her chest, she can’t breathe. People run down the street, everything is silent, only the sound of death rattle can be heard. Many passers-by are milling about in the middle of the streets, moving away from the facades, the windows are full of people leaning out, everyone is staring in bewildered terror, it’s seconds of anguish and desolation. Silence returns. It has passed. Nothing has collapsed, there is not a single crack. There are people in the square, lots of them, they come out of the houses, some are trembling, others are crying, some are hugging, others are kneeling, they are short of breath, sobbing, walking around lost. Lázaro sits up and embraces Nikoleta, she wraps her arms around him, unable to hold back a sudden sob.

― Nikoleta: Go downstairs to see Felisa, run! -the boy hurries down the stairs.

The woman is on the couch snoring. The boy laughs through a couple of tears.

― Lázaro: Qué fort, abuela! (This is too much, granny) -Nikoleta comes down the stairs-. She’s asleep, she hasn’t even noticed.

― Nik: It’s for the best… and how she snores… -he chuckles.

― Láz: Fotre… (fuck) What was that?

― Nik: I don’t know… -she goes up to him and pinches his cheek. But it was really really creepy -he grimaces at the last word.

They both pick up their phones and look at the notifications, no one says anything, nothing new, but suddenly the comments and videos start pouring in. There are thousands of them.

Ω

The echo of radio waves can be heard.

― This is Fog Heron from the shadows. Does anyone read me?….

This is Fog Heron from the shadows… -a very faint female voice, like a broken whisper, desperate and exhausted by the tireless repetition of her distress message.

…

The same sentence day after day, weaker and weaker, sadder and sadder, impotent.

Silence.

…

…

This is Fog Heron from the shadows. Does anyone read me?….

― This is Stone Turtle, I’VE GOT YOU!!!! -replies a male voice. He shudders up and down as he hears his companion, he is trembling, a mix of elation, fear and surprise.

― Heron: Oh… at last! God! Thank you! Thank you -she says almost broken by the effort, barely holding her breath. Turtle…

― Turtle: Quick, don’t go, hold on, tell me where you are. Come on, Heron! Give me coordinates!

― Heron: I’m trapped in a tourmaline, I can’t get out -she slurs, breathless. I got this in the shadows, you can hear me, but it leaves me lifeless wrecked- with trembling fingers she touches the contraption on her lap. With small objects I’ve accumulated over the past 29 years I built a simplex, I started broadcasting a year ago….

― Turtle: What a bastard! -he shouts as he rubs his forehead hard-. Wow, you have no coordinates -he exclaims helplessly.

― Heron: No…

― Turtle: Well… the important thing is that you are alive, you ARE ALIVE!…. -he’s engrossed, almost as if he’s standing in front of her, eyes wide open, stretching out his hand. I’m sorry I didn’t find you sooner.

― Heron: Tell me. Is my brother still alive?

― Turtle: Yes. Fire Hawk is still alive and isolated.

― Heron: Ooh… -a languid sigh-, he’s stopped trying, hasn’t he?

― Turtle: -holds his breath for a moment and barely utters- Yes. he tried, he worked at it day and night, but he gave up years ago.

― Heron: No -she mumbles in disgust-. What about you? What about them?

― Turtle: Neither… -he says with deep sadness, ashamed-, we have nothing left, Heron.

― Heron: And the rest ?

― Turtle: Nobody, Heron, there’s nobody, we’re extinct RRROOMMM! -a loud rumble shakes the communication, an eerie sensation, Turtle shudders. Heron?!

― Heron: I’m fine! It’s coming, I’ll get back to you -she hides the simplex behind his body.

― Turtle: HERON!!!

Ω

“…Asshasjkaabassh…” the restless whisper of the shadows can be heard in every corner. A female figure walks through the corridors towards the end of a diaphanous room, in its centre there is a black rock, the surface waves violently intersecting edges.

Beside it, a winged, humanoid figure emerges from the ground, blurred in form and features. On what appears to be the face, there is a glow that twinkles like two stars in the night. It bows to its mistress with a reverence.

― Woman: Search all of Valencia, let no stone be left unturned -she turns and looks intimidatingly at the winged shadow-. Search wherever they may be, get out of the capital as soon as you’ve covered it, I want you in every house, in every corner. Intercept every message on the network, every call, everything. Don’t come back without him -the shadow disappears, filtering into the ground.

The woman takes a small step towards the reflection returned by the tourmaline. As the woman calms her anger, the tourmaline reduces its many facets and sharp vertices. Her gaze searches the darkness of the mineral, delving into the depths that its distorted image returns to her. The completely flat surface becomes almost transparent.

― Woman: Jimena… -with a hoarse whisper-, Jimena -on the other side is the decrepit face of a middle-aged woman, ragged with dishevelled hair, their gazes meet. Jimena’s blue eyes quiver in the shadowed gaze of her captor.

― Jimena: Yes, Mérida -she replies from the darkness, her eyes narrowed and her chin raised.

― Mérida: They have stolen Chronos from me -she says phlegmatically.

― Jim: I’m glad, it was never yours -with a sardonic laugh.

― Mér: Don’t laugh at me, Jimena… -she drags out the words in a dismissive tone, Jimena folds her arms-. Chronos is conjured as if he were a part of my body, and I didn’t even notice when I lost him -she steps forward to close the distance-. You are bound to each of your creations -she sweeps her gaze over the other woman’s features-. Concentrate on the watch and tell me where it is -her pupils widen, his jaw clenches, letting the words slip out between her teeth-. If you don’t want me to…

― Jim: I HAVE BEEN IN THE SHADOWS FOR 29 YEARS!!!! -exclaims defiantly mustering with the little strength she has left in a desperate cry-. And my brother has lived in the body of an old crippled man for more than half his life. There is nothing you can threaten me with. You have no control over us -she tilts her face forward, her exhalation condenses on the thin glass that separates them-. You’ve never had it with me, that’s why I’m here -she lifts her hand to the level of their faces and scratches the glass with a shrill sound. Mérida watches her with a frown-. You can no longer play with my feelings or manipulate my thoughts. I am empty. Just like you.

― Mér: I don’t…

― Jim: Shut up! Don’t you think I can see how desperate and empty you are?! -her chest heaves.

― Mér: Jimena… -an almost inaudible whisper. She tilts her head forward, touching the glass with her forehead, Jimena hesitates for a second, finally mimics the gesture.

― Jim: Mérida, I can feel it, whether you like it or not -Mérida closes her eyes and slowly shakes her head without letting go of the touch.

― Mér: Jimena, stop it… -her eyes suddenly widen with rage, the tourmaline curls up. Jimena drops her body on the glass, resting her hands on the glass.

― Jim: You can’t kill someone who is already dead, as dead as you are -Mérida turns violently, enveloping Jimena in absolute darkness.

Ω

The morning is stormy grey. The networks, radio and television are buzzing with commentaries on the earthquake and the horrible feeling that has accompanied it.

― Turtle: Nélida, Salma, something incredible has happened -he says with a tense, jubilant gesture-. I’ve found her, Jimena is alive -the women exclaim with joy, covering their mouths so as not to scream.

― Nélida: Oh, Jimena! But that’s fantastic -she says, her voice cracking and her eyes flooding with tears of joy.

― Salma: Tell us what she said to you! -she says with her eyes wide open and her body bristling.

― Turtle: I barely had time to exchange a few words with her… -he rocks back on his heels. The little information he has to offer is oppressive. Finally he buries his hands in his trouser pockets-. She asked about her brother, and wanted to know about our plan to rescue her -his face breaks down-. We made contact just before the earthquake, which makes me think the worst.

― Nél: Roland, we have to get her out of there now. How do we do that?

― Roland: She will contact us again, we must be on the lookout for any sign.

― Nél: It has taken her 29 years to find a way to contact us, 29 years of struggling to achieve such a brief contact and we are impassive -he says with a broken, trembling voice, covering his mouth with his hand, biting his fingers-. Look at us, we are nothing… -he turns to look at his reflection in the mirror, which returns three disfigured, scarred figures.

― Sal: No, Nélida, what’s happening is that we’ve been telling each other this lie for almost three decades -she shakes angrily at her sister, showing the scars on her hands angrily, thrusting her face forward to reveal her collars-. Mérida can’t steal anything from us! -she angrily removes her wig and throws it to the ground. We’re engineers, it’s in our blood!

Ω

Nikoleta and Lázaro leave the house with a cloth bag in which they carry the stolen goods, she goes first. They go down the stairs, leave the doorway and walk through the narrow streets of the El Carmen neighbourhood. She stops for a moment to look at her mobile phone “Pi, Pi”, a notification, he takes a few steps ahead of her.

― Nikoleta: Hey, Lázaro! Come here, turn around, you’ve got something hooked on your fanny pack -the boy turns around, his fanny pack is behind him, half covered by his T-shirt, she takes the object in her hands-. Mooola (Cool), it looks like an antique pocket watch -she says as she holds it up to her eyes to see it in detail, it’s a small women’s watch, with an opaque button- it’s beautiful. I don’t think it’s valuable though, you must have bumped into someone in the market and it got caught.

― Lázaro: Fotre! (Fuck!) For once I’ve stolen it without wanting to and it’s a piece of junk, it doesn’t even show the time! -he says, looking sideways at the watch. She gives it to him to look at, he looks at it for a couple of seconds and gives it back to her-. Throw it in the fanny pack and take it to the old man, maybe he’ll give us something for it.

― Nik: It doesn’t look like it’s made in China or cheap. Just look at the engraving, it’s very nice, it says “Semper et in aeternum” (always and forever), besides, look at the dial, it doesn’t show the time, it marks something else -he raises a scrutinising eyebrow-. If the old man doesn’t give us anything, I’ll keep it.

― Láz: You can have it for me, I like it for you -he says, glancing intermittently at the watch and her friend-. But let’s get out of here, I need to get rid of everything we’ve stolen, it makes me feel bad after everything that’s happened today. I want to go home alone with the money.

“Pi, Pi”, again another notification. Nikoleta stops again, Lázaro looks at her impatiently, waiting for her. She walks towards him without looking up, turning up the volume on the device. They both watch the news playing on the smartphone.

“During this morning, the Guardia Civil, after more than three months investigating the pickpockets of the Mercado Central of Valencia, have seen how the possibility of success of their work vanished at last moment. These pickpockets have accumulated more than 500 complaints for theft so far this year, not forgetting those they had already accumulated during the previous year. They have become a real ordeal for visitors to the Mercado Central as well as for the vendors, who are fed up with these thieves. What has transpired, however, is that they are two young minors, a girl of around 17 and a boy of just 13. We are going to show in exclusive the images captured by the security cameras, where we can see how the agents chase the delinquents through the crowd, when they slink away in an astonishing way and immediately disappear”. The video ends. “However, the news to highlight today is the unexpected earthquake that has shaken the city of Valencia, geologists have not registered any epicentre, an earthquake that we have all felt, but that has not existed in any mechanical means of registration. Scientists can find no logical explanation for the phenomenon. From the Mercado Central of Valencia, Abril Onil for Canal Nuef, bona vesprada” (April Onil for channel Nine, good afternoon).

“Pip pip” several messages on Lázaro and Nikoleta’s mobile phone commenting on the video:

# “Jordi: Almost, huh? I’m looking for a new job for you! XDDDD”

# “Alina: Luckily your picture is blurred. If Lázaro gets caught! LOL!”

# “Saladine: :O head to the juvenile centre. Get another job!”

# “Diana: Nikoleta, there are no places left in Valencia for you. They will give you gashola (jail) for many years. The boy should go back to the xatarreria (junkyard) and you go back to the cashe (street), where you’re from…”

― Nikoleta: -she snorts angrily- GRRRAAAAA!!!! -she punches the air, Lázaro watches her.

― Lázaro: You’re not coming back, are you? -he says with a sad undertone.

― Nik: No, Lázaro -her eyes don’t meet the boy’s, it’s lost in memories. Prostituting me is not an option -she looks up now, determined, and present-. You ran away from the foster’s home to never return -he nods-, so do I. We’ll stay together, if we can’t keep stealing in Valencia, we’ll leave, Spain and Europe are at our feet. Get it straight! -a haughty smile mars her face.

― Láz: Aha… -he nods, staring into Nikoleta’s eyes, clenching his fists tightly.

― Nik: -she walks over and ruffles her hair, she lets out a relieved breath-. Let’s sell this -they both walk.

Ω

― Roland: What are you doing, Nélida?

― Nélida: I am looking for any information that will help us, I remember that in the Visigothic Liber Ludiciorum there are legal decrees against the use of black magic. There are alchemical containment formulas to prevent and contain necromancy. Her body is bent over a sprinkling of books on the table, her attention is focused on the largest of them.

― Rol: Magic is for magical bloodlines, we are engineers, we are technology -he says with bitter affection, moving to the woman’s side, brushing his shoulder lightly against hers-. Your sister’s drive is great, but not just any path will do.

― Nél: I know… -she puts a hand on the man’s-. But we can’t stay here -she says, standing up, her face twisted with helplessness. She closes the book-. Ahh! hum hum… -she remains engrossed as she caresses the cover of the book with her fingertips.

― Rol: What do you think? -it never ceases to amaze him that after all this time his wife is a real mystery to him.

― Nél: I’m a type 2 engineer, I’m not one for prophecy, Roland, but I’m not going to ignore the visions of another bloodline either. Remember that Edisa’s “Time-Line Relay” showed a dystopian future. I don’t want to, I refuse to be the last of our lineage -she turns on her heels, placing a hand on her chest and taking a few wandering steps-. MÉ-RI-DA LI-VIA BOU-DI-CCA -she punctuates each syllable-. He can hold our kind no longer -Salma peers through the doorway with a tenacious expression.

― Salma: Jimena and Alejo are the most talented people our lineage has seen in centuries, their inventions are unimaginable to us. The fact that Jimena has made contact 29 years later cannot be insignificant -she says with eyes full of sparkle-, it’s our time, now or never!

― Rol: It will take more than our will to achieve something. Let’s think this through calmly.

― Sal: Time is against us, it’s time to act -he gives her a pleading look-. We have access to the atzucac (alley) to communicate without being noticed, and a few old things we could repair.

Ω

― Old man: Should I look for new tenants for the market or are you planning to stay there after this morning? A dark-featured man in a djellaba is waiting for them on the other side of the counter. It’s a halal butcher’s shop, on the shelves there are pots, food, second-hand bikes, folding chairs, a dirty sofa, fans of different models, all for sale.

― Nikoleta: End Game, we almost got caught, propose it to whoever you want. Mercado Central is over lover -she says in a resigned Spanish accent-. Do you have something for us, Abdel?

― Abdel: I have acquaintances in Alicante, Mallorca, Seville…, in case you’re thinking of leaving, I can find you a dealer there -he pauses thoughtfully-. And I’ll tell you one thing, you’re the best pickpockets I’ve seen in my 40 years in the profession, but control that greed of the last few months if you don’t want to end up in prison. You’re quicker at pickpocketing than I am at putting up the goods.

― Lázaro: What? -he says in total disbelief, wrinkling his face-. But you’re the one asking for a minimum to work with you, what are you talking about? -Abdel slams the counter.

― Nik: Hey -he glances furtively at his friend, refocusing on the man-. It’s alright Abdel, it’s been a busy day. Look, we just want to give you the goods, OK?

― Láz: …Yes, well… -he says, turning around and moving his lips in a mocking manner.

― Abd: It’s alright, you get used to certain comments even from those you treat as your own children, anyway… For everything you have brought me I can give you 300€ in cash, if I get anything else I will give it to you when I place the goods.

― Nik: OK -she says, intoning each letter.

― Láz: Take a look at this -he strides to stand in front of the old man. He takes the watch out of his fanny pack, the man looks at him suspiciously, carefully reaching forward, puts on a monocular magnifying glass and looks at it in detail.

― Abd: Bihagi alhahimi… (what the hell?) I don’t know what it’s made of and I don’t know how big it is, but the workmanship is beautiful -from under the counter he pulls out a pistol-shaped device, then points at the clock with it and the gauges oscillate undecidedly “pip pip”-. Iron, gold, silver, zinc, blah, blah, blah, blah, this must be an alloy contaminated by a few metals, so it’s worth whatever the buyer wants to pay for it -he continues to look admiringly at the watch-. Anyway, given its design and the fact that we don’t know the brand, I’ll give you €10 for it -he raises his head to look at them, one eye disproportionately enlarged by the lens-. Aitafaqna? (deal?)

― Nik: -she’s leaning on the counter looking at the watch, then looks up at the old man- No, we’ve decided it’s my kind of thing. Something nice for someone nice -she smiles innocently, taking the object. He reaches out to caress Nikoleta’s wrist when she abruptly withdraws her hand.

― Abd: Of course, a pretty watch for a pretty girl -he smiles, showing yellowish, crooked teeth while his eyes darken obscenely.

― Láz: Old man! -the man returns his attention to the boy. Lázaro grabs his hand, which is still extended on the counter, and presses it against the surface. The two look at each other for a few seconds, until Nikoleta returns the attention back to her-. I’ll take this as a gift, eh, huh! -she picks up a stiletto knife from among the items scattered on the counter, letting it slide into her hand until she grasps it by the hilt-. The crazy things people bring to the market, right? -her frown frames a defiant look with a half-smile-. Now why don’t you tell us where can we find out more about the watch? -the man releases himself from Lázaro and rubs his hand as he watches them.

― Abd: Mmm… It’s been closed to the public for years, in Bany dels Pavessos street there’s an old antique shop on the corner, it looks abandoned, I don’t know if the owner is dead. Many years ago it was a hive of people with very peculiar tastes, and he traded in exquisite goods, he had a reputation for knowing everything about any object that came into his hands -he looks around with a disdainful grimace. He pauses and returns his attention back to the young men-. The candle… If you see a lit candle on a piece of furniture by the door, it’s open. Curious, isn’t it? Once when I was waiting to be attended, I ran my hand over the flame -he raises his voice sharply, startling them-. It didn’t burn!… Anyway, I imagine that if he’s still alive, there’s a candle burning. If you can get him to come to the door, show him the watch quickly so that he pays attention to you, if he sees you near his shop looking like that, he’ll call the police.

― Láz: …okay.

Ω

― Jimena: Here Fog Heron to Stone Turtle! Here Fog Heron to Stone Turtle! -silence.

― Nélida: Here Storm Deer! At my side, Stone Turtle and Wind Fox -the voice choked with emotion.

― Jim: I don’t have time. Shadow Owl has lost Chronos, we have a chance as long as she doesn’t have him in her possession. Find him and hand him over to Fire Hawk, he’ll know what to do.

― Nél: Garza! Garza!? -the communication has been cut off but she holds the microphone in front of her lips-. We have to find that watch -her voice is now emphatic.

Salma leaves the room with a vengeance.

― Roland: Where are you going?

Ω

― Lázaro: Xé, quin fastic!!! (what a bummer!) -he says, standing up suddenly.

― Nikoleta: What is it?

― Láz: I know you love the watch, but all of this makes me tired and the watch makes me tired. We’ll throw it in the river and I’ll give you a really cool one? -he stretches out his arms and smiles a forced smile.

― Nik: Do you notice anything strange too? -she looks at him carefully, tilting her head so that their eyes are at the same level.

― Láz: …Yes? -he grimaces, visibly uncomfortable.

― Nik: Yes… -she whispers thoughtfully-. Here’s what we are going to do, we go to the antique shop, if the guardian of the crypt is there he can enlighten us with his wisdom and if we can’t find him, we throw it into the river… or not. She moves forward, leaving the boy behind.

― Láz: But… -he runs the distance that separates them quickly to catch up with her. She stops suddenly, placing a hand on his shoulder.

― Nikoleta: I want to know what it is.

Already in Bany dels Pavessos’ street, the route turns, restaurants, second-hand clothes shops, several grocery shops, there is almost nothing left of the old shops. After about 50 metres, you can see the sign hanging half-gnawed, “Anticuari Edetanorum”. The window’s shop is dirty and empty, full of dust. The sign on the door says “closed” and there are several large stickers reading “for sale” and “for rent”.

― Láz: Closed and no bell, can we go now?

― Nik: No -pushes the door but it doesn’t open, tries again.

― Láz: Let’s go, Nikoleta.

― Nik: Open it.

― Láz: No.

― Nik: Come on… -the boy snorts, pulls out a lock pick and opens the door with a gesture.

― Láz: That watch is not worth the trouble we’re getting into.

― Nik: -the young woman pushes the crank, half of her body is showing-. Hello, is anybody there? -she exclaims in a shrill voice.

― Male voice: HOW THE FUCK?!!!! CAN’T YOU READ!!!? -he says from inside the shop-. The shop is closed, go away! -Nikoleta looks at Lázaro with amusement, arching her eyebrows and making a U with her lips. The man walks fiercely towards the door to see who it is, whoever it is that has been able to break through the obstacles that protect the entrance.

― Nik: -Nikoleta has her arm raised towards the man, the watch hanging from her hand- Look at it! -she hesitates at the old man’s impetus, which is at odds with his fragile appearance.

The long, white-haired, dusty-looking, dirty-robed nonagenarian rushes over to get the boys out of the shop, but once he sees the watch he throws his bony hands towards the object as his mouth and eyes widen in utter disbelief. He stops abruptly, looks at them with absolute distrust, slowly turns and looks at the candle, the flame is completely horizontal. His face breaks down, but there is a strange half-smile that makes the youngsters recoil in fright.

― Antiquarian: Enter immediately!!! -he says, pushing them inside, clumsily entering, bumping into each other-. Go to the office, the door at the back. Come on! Don’t say anything until we’re inside.

― Láz: What are you on about, you old fossil?! Nikoleta, where are you going? He’s about to die, he doesn’t care about anything.

― Antiquarian: Shut up, I’m telling you! Let’s go to the office -the old man takes the remote control out of his pocket, presses the button and the metal shutters come down, hiding the shop windows and the entrance door. A sharp bang, it’s the padlocks locking everything. The room is darkened by the candlelight that bathes the room.

― Láz: MA MARE, TIA! (my mother! girl) -he just locked us up! Say something!

― Nik: Ssshhh! This just keeps getting better and better -Lázaro looks back at her in astonishment.

The old man enters the office, pushing them inside.

― Antiquarian: The watch, on the table, hurry up -Nikoleta carefully puts it on the table-. Where did you get it? -he says quietly to himself while rubs his face, sways stumbling against the wall, holding on to the door frame, dazed with emotion.

― Láz: Pufff… well… It must have got caught in the fanny pack while we were walking around the market this morning -he says nervously.

― Antiquarian: In the Mercado Central? That bastard was buying corpses… -he stares nowhere, lost in his thoughts. He picks up the watch between his tanned, ink-stained fingers. He gazes at it with fascination and fear. He puts it down on the table, looks up and watches them-. You don’t know what you’ve done, who are you? How did you do it?

― Láz: I don’t know, I really don’t know, it got stuck in my fanny pack, I didn’t mean to steal it -he looks at Nikoleta with a frown, Nikoleta shrugs.

― Antiquarian: Bullshit, it’s impossible, it can’t be separated from its owner -he puts his hand to his mouth in awe. He rubs his face. He reaches into a drawer and pulls out a box full of metal letters for printing, from the same drawer he takes out two sheets of yellowish, rough paper. Then he picks up an ink roller on the table-. Child, give me your hand.

― Láz: No!

― Antiquarian: Grrr… -he growls inwardly, closing his eyes to contain his anger-. Fucking kids… -he mumbles to himself.

Nikoleta looks at Lázaro, holding back a smile. Lázaro looks at her in disbelief, he doesn’t understand what it is that she finds so amusing.

― Antiquarian: You, mañaco (impertinent child), what’s your name? -he clenches his fists, looks back at the old man and clenches his jaw-. Very well, Mute boy… What about you? -the old man now looks at Nikoleta.

― Nik: What is your name? -Nikoleta looks at the old man still with a smile in her eyes. Lifting her head up, she looks firm.

― Antiquarian: Fuck me, my name is Alejo! A-LE-JO. Fu-ck-me, Alejo!!! -Nikoleta and Lázaro step back. They look at each other with their faces unhinged and a second later a stifled laugh breaks the tension of the moment.

― Alejo: What the fuck are you laughing at? -he looks at them intermittently with a stern gesture.

― Nik: My name is Nikoleta.

― Láz: I’m Lázaro.

― Ale: It wasn’t that hard, dammit!

― Ale: Now, give me your hand -Lázaro has his arms clenched at his sides and his fists are clenched-. Come on! I’m not going to hurt you, if you have the watch it’s because you are… -the old man shakes his head-. It’s important that you do as I say. You’ve come for the watch, haven’t you? -Lázaro reluctantly obeys, offering him his open palm, the old man passes him the roller, smudging it.

― Láz: What the…?

― Ale: Now reach into the box, take as many letters as you can fit in your fist and squeeze once -Alejo’s eyes lock with Lázaro’s-. Don’t move them between your fingers! -the boy nods-. Then release them and place your hand on this paper so that your imprint remains on it -he gestures with his hand on the table slowly, instructing the boy with his eyes still fixed on him-. Don’t use force! Just let it rest on its own weight, do you understand? -Lázaro nods again-. But stop looking at me and look at this!

― Láz: Yes, I’m going to! -he lowers his head stiffly, watching his own hand, and does so. Alejo nods as he sees that Lázaro has done it correctly.

― Ale: Now you, Nikoleta -the girl repeats the process. Alejo picks up the sheets of paper and looks at the prints, seeming to read something in the mess of letters and fingerprints-. You fools! -he exclaims with the surprise of having discovered something extraordinary-. BROOOMMMMM!!!! -a rattle that makes the objects on the shelves shake, the candle in the office flickers violently-. BASTARDS!!! They’re looking for us, we don’t have much time -the nervous man leans towards the young men, lowering his voice as if it were a secret-. I’ll tell you quickly. You are high level mentalists, that’s why you have achieved such a feat without her noticing it… -he says with a great emotion between surprise and admiration-. Let’s go back to the watch, this invention is the work of my sister, Jimena Alcabre Tacoronte. This watch is the Chronos´s heart, capable of stopping the advance of time in the body of the person who possesses it, making him or her remain young and immortal -he looks at his parchment-like hands-. My sister’s work is completed by my invention, The God´s word, everything that is written on its blank pages, happens. The problem with my book is that it doesn’t keep track of time, what is asked for could happen tomorrow or in a decade, so my sister added several hands to her watch to control the wills written -he runs his thumb caressing the surface of the sabonette-. We synchronised both inventions by combining our skills, and merging our creations was the worst decision of our lives -he says as he pulls a small key from a drawer-. This has belonged to Mérida Livia Boudicca -his face darkens-, but it will no longer do so -he inserts the key into a small hole in the back of the watch and the hands lose their compass, spinning meaninglessly-. If there is a god, today he has given us a chance, this key breaks the sequence of the clock and makes it self-destruct, in just a few hours its mechanism will collapse and there is no way to fix it… -he leans towards the clock and mumbles- Fuck you, motherfucker! -the youngsters lean towards him, expectantly, as if the clock is going to answer the old man. On the next table there are jewellery tools, a blowtorch and a small melting glass, he puts the key into the glass, lights the blowtorch with the lighter, but the flame is not normal, it spirals with green and blue colours. The key melts and evaporates at the touch of the fire-. It is Mordor’s fire -he says, seeing the surprised faces of the youngsters-, I invented it to destroy the metal with which my sister and I created our inventions -from a shelf he reaches for a book and a piece of paper on which he writes down an address.

The flame of the candle spins in every direction, he looks at it, holding his breath, his eyes bulging. You must run -he says breathlessly- go in that direction -he holds out his trembling hand, handing them the paper-. Take the watch and this facsimile of the verses, on it is written everything that is written in the original. Do not look back, do not hesitate, in your hands is the fate of the world -he pushes an armchair in the corner of the room, revealing a small door-, and say that the Fire Hawk sent you! RUN TO THE END! -he thrusts the blade, opening the passageway. Nikoleta and Lázaro look at each other in astonishment, the boy shakes his head and the young woman nods. Then they are crossing the threshold. The door closes with a heavy thud, the boys run without knowing where to, scared, hunched over, in the dark. The old man takes a seat in the armchair and lights a cigarette as he listens a presence creeping slowly through the shop, in the doorway a faceless humanoid figure appears, an opaque darkness, it wears a cloak that looks like a pair of wings folded back brushing the ground, it says nothing but with its hand signals the old man gets up. The old man takes a deep puff on his cigarette as he gets up, the figure approaches him, turns his face towards him and gets closer within a couple of centimetres of touching him. He pulls back the armchair with his back to the old man, looking for the secret access through which the smell of the clock oozes. Alejo gently blows the cigarette smoke over the figure, the cloud is drawn with broken lines that rest on the shadow, making it completely immobile.

― Ale: Very good for not having tried it before, it’s a success, ha ha ha ha -he laughs looking at the cigar-. You know…, you’re lucky it’s not an ordago, it leaves them immobile forever and ever. You… -he reaches out a hand towards the shadow’s wings but stops short. You are a living shadow…

Ω

Flight to the Club of the One-Eyed Poets

Mérida stands in front of the tourmaline, her body leaning forward, tired, her perly face with sweat.

― Mérida: Jimena -she pronounces her name with an icy undertone. The sharp facets turn flat, revealing the other side.

― Jimena: -the woman rises from the floor, when she senses her weakness she rushes over to her- What’s wrong? -concern seeps into her voice.

― Mér: I’m afraid the watch is in your brother’s hands by some strange chance -she slurs, gritting her teeth-, and he has inexplicably tampered with the mechanism, rendering it useless. And not only that, but the watch is still missing -she leans against the tourmaline, seeking refuge in Jimena’s eyes. She has never felt this physical weakness before, as if life was abandoning her body.

― Jim: -looks up and down at the other woman. Her eyes widen, her eyebrows twitch almost together and her mouth lets out a convulsive moan- What have you done Mérida, what have you done, look at you!

― Mér: Nothing, it was your idiot brother! -she slaps her other hand on the tourmaline splashing with sweat.

― Jim: It was you, you started all this and look at you!

― Mér: -her eyes, alienated by weakness, become crystalline- If you were in my shoes you would have done the same… -Jimena clenches her fists and hits the tourmaline with all her might… CLACK! Jimena looks on thinking she has broken the crystal. Mérida laughs, shaking her head- Really, Jimena? -Jimena looks at her visibly annoyed.

― Jim: -a tear slips down her cheek- I would have never done this to you -Mérida turns away, resting her back on the stone, hiding her own tears from Jimena’s view. The darkness envelops Jimena again-. Who is it that’s really locked up? -her voice is distant, drowned out by the ore. Mérida slides across the surface to the ground-. That clock is beyond repair, once the decompass is activated, there’s no going back, that’s how it was created. Your only option is to destroy the clock and go back to being mortal -Mérida looks over her shoulder at the tourmaline. Jimena’s voice is closer now-. Destroy it and live the time you have left.

Mérida sits hesitantly in front of the ore and the image of Jimena is visible again, she is on her knees.

― Mér: Living without the watch is not an option, Jimena. Tell me, what do you want in exchange for a new Chronos? -Jimena slowly sits up, her eyes burning.

― Jim: Have you forgotten that you broke into our workshop, that you stole our inventions, destroyed all my notes and studies in order to become the great Empress -a hoarse laugh accentuates her indignation-. I haven’t touched a watch in so many years that I couldn’t even change batteries.

― Mér: I don’t believe you. You are here because you are part of the prophecy, along with your brother you are mestizo, you are the zenith of engineers and travellers.

― Jim: …No, Mérida… I’m not the engineer I once was, I don’t even know who I am at this point… and I owe it all to you -she closes her eyes and swallows loudly, Mérida awaits expectantly-. The game is over. Destroy the watch.

― Mér: I DON’T HAVE THE WATCH!!! -she exclaims helplessly. Her whole body reverberates with denial.

― Jim: You have to find it and destroy it! -Jimena’s eyes dart from side to side looking for answers-. I don’t know how to fix it! But if you destroy it, you’ll live -her gaze stops on Mérida’s hands-. Mérida…no.

― Mér: I’m getting old… Look at me!!!! -she exclaims, choked with dread. All the edges of the tourmaline soften and the surface becomes translucent. Jimena crosses the glass wall and grabs her hands.

― Jim: Mérida… I don’t want you to die -she clenches her hands as she cries.

― Mér: Jimena… -she whispers as her voice cracks-. Maybe you’re right and I should destroy the watch -the Empress’s henchmen suddenly appear, dragging Alejo with them, pushing him forward, he falls to the ground face first, his nose bursting, his beard bloody. Mérida releases her hands from Jimena’s and rushes vehemently towards Alejo.

― Alejo: -he’s face down on the floor, bending his back in an attempt to sit up. He turns his head to look at Mérida- You’re getting old… Ha ha ha ha ha… -Mérida kneels down next to him and grabs him by the collar of his shirt.

― Mér: Give me back my watch -she says emphatically.

― Ale: No shit? -he says with a huff-. His Majesty has lost the watch? -his eyesight is blurred and he is unable to focus it.

― Mér: I know you know where it is. No one but you could have mismatched it.

― Ale: Noooo! Torture me and pluck my skin. Watching you rot and become impotent, it will be fun, believe me -at a wave of his hand the shadows lift the old man, who remains suspended inert.

― Jim: Mérida, nooo!!!!! -Jimena runs towards them. Mérida turns, her face is disfigured with anger. Jimena hits one of the henchmen standing in front of her.

― Mér: WHERE IS IT?

― Ale: YOU DIE!!!! -he raises his arms to strangle her but she is quicker and squeezes the old man’s flaccid neck.

Jimena rushes at Mérida using the weight of her body, knocking her down and landing on top of her. She straddles Mérida, who for a second tries to break free from Jimena’s grip but stops. Jimena takes a quick breath, Mérida watches her expectantly, releases one of her arms from Jimena’s grip and slowly brings her hand up to her face, caressing her cheek gently. Jimena hesitates, a moment passes as she slowly moves closer to her face. She brings his lips to her ear.

― Jim: It’s time, Mérida. We can stop all this… You and I -Mérida closes her eyes, letting a couple of tears fall.

One of the shadows knocks Jimena to the ground, freeing Mérida who gets up hastily, looking in the direction in which the woman has fallen, grunts motionless on the ground, clutching her side, cringing in pain. Mérida turns to the shadow, walks towards it with a feral gesture, grabs it by the neck, extinguishing it in ashes.

Alejo lies on the floor dismembered, bubbling in his own blood trying to breathe, he lets out a laugh that turns into a cough.

― Ale: YOU! BITCH IN LOV… -Mérida’s foot slams hard into Alejo’s mouth, knocking out a couple of teeth.

Ω

As night falls the army of darkness stirs in search of the watch, there is not a single Tinddaya in all of Valencia walking without the shadow’s vigilance.

― Nélida: …Do you feel it? It’s suffocating -she says, looking out of the window-. The house is surrounded.

― Salma: -turns her head, paying attention to something coming from outside the house- They have entered.

― Roland: The quick brown fox jumps over the lazy dog -the man whispers what seems to be a signal, they stop when they hear the phrase.

The three of them look carefully at the place where the shadows that are already wandering around the house are. Roland rushes to the fireplace, stands in the corner, lights his pipe and takes a puff that fills the room with smoke, then reaches for the handle hidden in the mouth of a bronze dragon framing the fireplace, and with a subtle flick of his finger, operates it. The lights of the chandelier flicker for a moment, the three figures in the room remain almost motionless, breathing, blinking, but no longer themselves. However, something moves on the circular mirror hanging on the wall, the reflection has come to life. The illusion of the real plane allows them to work in a room hidden from the shadows.

― Sal: -looking in the mirror, checking that everything is working on the other side, she looks on, puffed up, with a smile- They’re here, Carol Anne!!!! -she gets back to where her sister and Roland are.

Ω

Nikoleta and Lázaro continue to make their way forward.

― Lázaro: 12%, we are about to run out of electricity, my mobile phone battery is dying, we’ve been walking for over an hour. I told you it wasn’t a good idea.

― Nikoleta: I know, I know, the 9 1/3 station roll has got to me -she says with a tired look on her face-. I’m getting dizzy and I can’t breathe -she’s getting nervous-. Lázaro, hurry up, I don’t want to stay in the dark here -she grabs him by the shirt and pulls him along, speeding up the pace.

― Láz: What if he’s crazy and has set us up? We’re going to die -he cries, provoking his friend.

― Nik: -looks at her friend stunned, the thought of it chills her to the bone- WE’RE GOING TO DIE, LÁZAROOO!!!!!

― Láz: Take it easy, NIKOLETA!!! BREATHE! -he exclaims, pulling her so that she doesn’t stop-. We are going to live! Yes, let’s live! -she runs after him.

Several metres later she stops.

― Láz: Are you OK?

― Nik: Yep -the boy nods and they continue.

PUUUUMMM!!!!!

― Láz: Ouuuch!!!! -he exclaims after hitting the wall-. Ñas coca!! (what a bump) - he puts his hand on his forehead to relieve the pain.

― Nik: Did you hurt yourself?

― Láz: Aha… -he says with a crooked gesture.

― Nik: This is finally over!

― Láz: Yes, I didn’t see it coming. Look, a door -Lázaro turns the knob and pushes the door carefully, he peeks out-. Can you feel it? This is when they catch us and torture us -he whispers-, it’s an alley, this is the end then.

― Nik: Stinky Pinky… -she says, raising a finger to warn Lázaro.

― Láz: Mimi mimimi… -he mocks her.

― Nik: -she looks at him seriously, he nods- Where are we going now?

― Láz: Well, this is it… The note says 864 Atzúcac de la Calle de la Salud. Knock three times on the door -they walk to the end of the alley, on one side there’s a wall and on the other side a gate.

― Nik: But what door? There is nothing.

― Láz: Look, there’s a squiggle painted on the wall that looks like a door.

Lázaro approaches and knocks three times, knock, knock, knock. Both are left expectant.

― Nik: …Lázaro … -she whispers-, the door we came through is gone -Lázaro approaches her, whispering with his finger on his lips.

― Láz: Listen -footsteps on the other side of the drawn door, someone looks through the peephole, the porthole opens, a man’s eyes appear on the other side.

― Man: Who are you and how did you get here? Quickly!

― Láz: Nikoleta and Lázaro, the Fire Hawk sends us, we have Chronos and a facsimile of the Verses -he says hurriedly, losing his breath.

― Man: OH MY GOD! -the man opens the door quickly and pushes them into the house; he looks out, observes and closes it; he turns a huge submarine-like key-. It’s already sealed, hurry up the stairs, let’s go!

My name is Roland, I’m a friend of Alejo’s -he says, coming up the stairs behind them-. And no doubt you are the cause of this commotion -he passes between the two young friends, standing in front of the door, the light filtering underneath it, when he opens it two women look at them in astonishment-. Nélida, Salma, Alejo sent them to us, they have the watch and the facsimile.

― Nélida: IT’S NOT TRUE!!!! -she says, putting a hand on her chest and approaching the children. Lázaro takes the watch out of his fanny pack and the book from under his shirt and offers it to her, the woman takes it in her hands-. I can’t believe it, look at the watch… it’s out of adjustment -Salma stands up, looks at the watch and with a smile approaches the youngster.

― Salma: How did you steal it?

― Láz: I don’t know, I didn’t mean to…

― Sal: Nooh… impossible, it’s conjured -the woman looks at the device carefully and then looks up, staring into Nikoleta’s eyes-. You are… Ha ha ha. You’re both mentalists and you don’t know it, do you? -she laughs in amusement.

― Rol: Salma, keep your voice down! -he nods, feigning seriousness.

― Nél: Mentalists with no small amount of Teraghe`n to steal the watch from the Darkness’ Empress -she says, smiling complacently-, you must be surprised by all this. We owe you an explanation.

― Nik: eeeeEEHH… NO, I don’t know if I want to hear any more, not to be rude, but I think you should go to sleep -she takes a few steps backwards, laughing nervously. She puts her hands on Lázaro’s shoulders.

― Sal: Do you really want to leave without knowing who you are? -she tilts her head to one side when Nikoleta turns to look at her.

― Nik: This is crazy -she pouts.

― Sal: You’re absolutely right. But you know what we’re talking about, you’ve always known you’re different -he walks around the room very slowly without losing eye contact.

― Nik: Yes… eee… any teenager feels like that -she states it as a truism.

― Sal: In this case it goes further. This sensation -she lifts a finger and silently stares into the void in abstraction-, is the vibration that Mérida gives off in search of the watch. Both of you can feel it only because you are not human, you are not Yamnaya. It is your nature that has allowed you to steal this from Mérida without her noticing, which is extraordinary, and she was unaware of your existence. She has us and all Tinddaya subjugated to her will and our abilities reduced to nothing -she raises his hand and shows them a black ring-. This is how she rules us. But even with this ring, we are who we are -she raises his other hand, open, palm up, and says in a different tone of voice, Tihulan, Tinddaya, ayt Naga (greetings Tinddaya, children of the Naga) -a drawing under the skin appears in a faint, flickering light. Roland pronounces the words and shows his, Nélida does the same-. This is the symbol of our lineage.

― Láz: -he takes a few steps towards Roland and looks down at his hand. He looks at it carefully, going over the skin, the arm, the neck, until he reaches Roland’s eyes- The scars, all three of you have them.

― Rol: These scars are the punishment Mérida inflicted on us for being her best friends and not supporting her madness. She burned our skin with her shadows -Roland approaches the lamp and increases the intensity of the light, the young men look perplexed at the magnitude of the punishment, their faces are disfigured-. Alejo is not an old man, he is our age, but 29 years ago Mérida cursed him and aged him in an instant.

And as for this house, we have been living here for 29 years, locked up, unable to get out, at least until a couple of years ago when we managed to open a breach and connect with the atzucac (hidden alley) through which you have entered here. We could escape, but no reason was worth enough the risk until you came here.

― Láz: I don’t understand… -he stutters in bewilderment.

― Sal: You are thieves, right?

― Láz: …aha…

― Sal: The police never caught you, your faces are blurred on camera, etc., aren’t they?

― Láz: …Ehm… well… not today… but in general yes…

― Sal: There you go! -she says, folding her arms in delight.

― Rol: Nikoleta, Lázaro -he interrupts with a serious air-, this is my wife Nélida and this is her sister Salma. I understand that you are astonished and that you don’t believe what we are telling you. The world is much more complex than you have been led to believe; however, we don’t have time for chitchat, if you are mentalists I beg you to help us take down Mérida. Any help is much needed -Roland stands expectantly watching them, Nikoleta stands by the door nailed to the floor watching, Lázaro takes a few steps back to stand beside her-. I’ll take that as a yes.

― Sal: I would expect nothing less -he says, shrugging his shoulders with a smile.

― Nik: No… it’s not a no, but it’s not a yes. The thing is that even though this is a very cool thing to do -he says with a one-eyed, puzzled look on his face-. Mmmm… I think it’s best if we go home and come back tomorrow. We won’t say anything to anyone, be sure of it -the girl turns and takes Lázaro by the hand, pulling him along.

― Rol: Nikoleta, Lázaro, don’t leave. We need you.

― Nél: Jimena and Alejo are our family, we have to save them -she says, saddened-. Please help us… Please -her voice breaks and tears well up in her eyes.

― Láz: …OK… -Lázaro looks at her tenderly.

― Nik: Really? -she looks at Lázaro astonished.

― Láz: I want to help them, if you want to, of course.

― Nik: -she looks at him for a few seconds, her gesture softens- OK, if we die it’ll be your fault -she moves away from the door-. I just wanted to know something about that watch, not catch horrocruxes -Nikoleta approaches the mirror, looks at her reflection, her image is not reflected, the three tenants are the only ones on the other side, almost motionless. She turns around with a twisted gesture and points her finger, Lázaro watches carefully.

― Nél: We are on the other side of the glass in a room contained within the wall. There are several spaces created inside the walls, what from the outside is flat and uninhabitable, with a bit of mathematics becomes large spaces, so we have been able to reach the atzucac (hidden alley). What you see on the other side is a hologram, the acolytes of Mérida are watching us, they think we are still in the hall doing the only thing that can be done in this house, nothing. But now we are here, with a unique opportunity -a huge smile crosses her face and she looks tenderly at Lázaro putting her hands on her chest.

― Rol: All right! Nélida, Salma, we have two options: we destroy the watch, and without it she lives, she submits to us for the rest of her time left, or we let it spin until it collapses, destroying the book with her and the Empress, but with the sacrifice of all those who appear written in it -she says, interlacing her fingers nervously.

― Nik: Are people going to die if we don’t destroy it?

― Nél: Yes, the numerous deaths of politicians and people with economic power are her work, she seeks greater power by killing those who reveal themselves to her, she rules in many areas worldwide from the shadows, but she is still not all-powerful. And she will kill anyone if she thinks they will do something in the future that will hinder her saying.

― Láz: Then we destroy it.

― Sal: We should destroy it, yes -he looks at Roland-, but if we do it now we will still be subjugated to an even more bloodthirsty Empress.

― Nik: If your life depends on that watch, will you kill anyone to get it back?

― Rol: Exactly, and that’s why you’re here. She doesn’t know you and you can easily run away from her. We will be watching everything and you will count with our inventions to carry out your task, do you agree?

― Nik: What if something goes wrong?

― Nél: I know you are afraid, so are we, all Tinddaya, our species lives in fear, that’s why we want to put an end to this -she says with a gesture that combines weariness and hope-. It is not by chance that you got the watch -she stays silent for a moment-. We need you to be absolutely sure of what you’re going to do -she looks at them expectantly, both affirm.

― Sal: Good! -he dances for a few seconds, barely moving from his seat, grinning from ear to ear.

― Nél: I’ll search on the facsimile for possible relevance victims, if the watch is out of sync, death will follow one after the other without meaning.

― Nik: Are you going to decide who lives and who doesn’t? -she asks perplexed.

― Rol: We have no choice, we will save those who are decisive for the story.

― Nik: That’s very unfair, you don’t know what will happen in the future.

― Nél: -approaches Nikoleta- That’s not entirely true, the future is written every day on thousands of existing options and each option gives rise to other options. The fascinating thing is to pay attention to the little variability to which it all sums up -she opens her arms to embrace the space and loses her gaze-. Mérida kills to gain power and also to avoid losing it -she points to the book, tapping it with her finger-. One way or another, when someone might be a risk to her, the most practical thing to do is to make them disappear to avoid the occasion. Look -she says, tapping her finger on a list in the book-. She plans to kill Tesla’s great-granddaughter at the next renewable energy convention in Valencia, and a certain Jean-Jacques Ampère next year, when he is hired by Cartagena’s Polytechnic -she mentions them, understanding them as important people-. Oh, NO, she has marked Neil Tyson to die at the very moment he discovers how to contact…. -an interrupted sigh, she pauses in grief, holding her hand to her chest in suffocation, tears in her eyes-. Oh, Roland, she knows this because of our Alejandra… we have done nothing for her.

― Rol: -rubbing his face helplessly, he looks at the ground- Shit… I hadn’t noticed the girl.

― Sal: We have to kill that witch mercilessly, now.

― Rol: Save as many as we can and let Mérida fall.

― Nik: … Why don’t we destroy the book? It’s right in front of us -she asks incredulously.

― Nél: This is a facsimile, a copy, it has no power, it just shows us everything she writes in the original. At this moment we can only destroy the watch.

― Rol: If there is nothing more to discuss, sit down with me while I prepare a couple of tools and bring you up to date on who we are -Nélida and Salma prepare their own work. Roland, already with tools in hand, as he works, begins to to speak-. Our species is the Tinddaya and our origins date back to the dawn of this planet as we know it today.

We are “children” of an extraterrestrial species, the Naga, who in order to generate manpower to extract the natural resources of this planet, combined their genetics with terrestrial creatures to give rise to a creature capable of obeying orders and serving them, thus appeared the Yamnaya, or as they call themselves, humans.

The Naga were fiercely belligerent, and proxy wars eventually wiped them out. The few Naga on earth, with no planet to return to, decided to perpetuate their own species, took the Yamnaya, and from them created the Tinddaya, equal to humans, but with many of the Naga’s abilities. Unimaginable abilities and divided into lineages. Abilities that depend on Teraghe-n-Magheq, universal energy that we do manipulate, while the Yamnaya are unaware of it. As you have heard Nélida say before, Teraghe`n is easier to say.

This difference between us and humans led to many wars, and our final decision, after being depleted as a species, was to hide, hence we are known as the forgotten ones, the Oblivium.

― Láz: Que foooort!! (this is too much) -he says in rapt attention. Nikoleta keeps her mouth open and her eyebrows raised.

― Rol: -Roland leans against the fireplace and looks at his reflection, which remains motionless on the other side next to the hologram of Salma and Nélida- After a bit of classical history, it’s time for the contemporary era -he clears his throat with an uncomfortable frown-. Mérida, Jimena and Alejo, whom you know, are the mestizos, Tinddaya of superior powers than the rest. Their inventions are partly the cause of this present situation, but most of all, Mérida’s necromantic idiosyncrasies. The necromancers are dark and are a different lineage from the others. They want to break the masquerade, to be visible to humans and to dominate over them. And that is not viable for the rest of Tinddaya. Mérida rose to power with a dictatorship that was made possible by the inventions of the siblings, Jimena and Alejo, and by an invention of the Empress herself, the fallacious ring -he raises his hand and shows it-, with the one who governs us all.

― Láz: What are your skills? -he looks at Roland, who is adjusting a camera, at Nélida, who is still writing the list, and he stops at Salma, who is busy under a very curious sewing machine moving some fabrics on what looks like a sewing pattern.

― Sal: -she turns and lifts up the garments to dress the young men, with a smile- I am the Coco Chanel of the Tinddaya. Well… I was -she says wistfully.

― Rol: Nélida is a master of musical scores, her music boxes are capable of anything. Salma makes clothes that can be used for absolutely anything, and I take pictures, pictures like you have never seen in the human world.

― Nik: And what about Jimena and Alejo?

― Rol: They are engineers and travellers, they created a watch that made you immortal and a book where everything you write comes true. The watch is in your hand, and regarding the book, we have the facsimile. With something like that you can dominate the world -he turns his head towards Lázaro and Nikoleta, their eyes still fixed on him, as if trying to take in all this new information.

To achieve all that power, in addition to committing countless crimes, she locked up Jimena, turned Alejo into an old man and completely disfigured us by burning us.

A few seconds pass in which the man is silent, concentrating deeply on finishing what he is doing. The women are also busy finishing their work.

― Rol: The camera is ready and the music box is ready too, I would say, it is not my invention, but I know it so well that I have dared to start it up again -he puts down the screwdriver and looks at the boys with satisfaction-. These are gadgets from the 90s, it would be easier to give you a phone with apps, but this is the way it is. The crank operates the music box and the camera works by clicking here, the reel is changed like this -he shows it so they can see how it’s done-, and you’ll have to use one reel per shot, each reel holds five people. I’ll give you four, I don’t have any more.

― Nél: Just one more minute -she says without taking her eyes off from the book.

― Sal: Come and I’ll adjust it for you -the friends approach him and get dressed in what looks like overalls. Salma presses an almost imperceptible button on the collar and the garment fits their bodies perfectly-. Look at the command on the wrist, button up and down to choose from the list what you want the suit to do, go unnoticed, stand out, dress up, street wear, multi-style, etc, it’s easy.

― Nik: What is multi-style?

― Sal: Every person who looks at you sees a different dress. That way no one can identify you by your clothing -the youngsters nod with a smirk-. The suit is flame-retardant, it’s reinforced to withstand 100 kilos of impact, it’s temperature-regulating so you won’t freeze to death, it’s also breathable and self-cleaning. To take it off, press the button on the collar again.

― Nél: The events are executed randomly, there is no way to predict their actions. Also, I can confirm that the watch and the book are synchronously linked, if one gets destroyed, the other gets destroyed as well. The ink is being erased, which means that not only is it not working properly, but it is self-destructing, which has prevented me from producing a more complete list. The book will collapse along with the clock in a matter of a few hours, possibly it won’t even last until the early hours of the morning. If we keep it away from Mérida during that time, tomorrow it will all be over.

I have searched only for those people we have in Valencia, as well as their place of residence and work, I hope there are no setbacks, in case of not finding someone, there will be no choice but to move on to the next one. The first name is Dana McKully, she is pregnant with a girl from the Sensitive lineage, then Ekaitz Aramburu, he hasn’t done anything yet, but in the future he will be one of the most important geneticists for us, he is a galenician. And finally Andrea Nicolai Tesla, Nicola Tesla’s great-granddaughter, an engineer with greater abilities than her great-grandfather. These three names must be saved. All the others will die -she closes her eyes for a second and bites her lips-. You know how much time you have, not a minute more, not a minute less.

― Rol: Use the music box to hypnotise them and then take a photo of them. Remember, one photo per reel, each photo can hold a group of five people, you can save the whole family if necessary. They will disappear from the real picture as they’ll be taken and kept inside the film, if the film breaks, they die. As long as they are on the film, reality does not affect them.

― Sal: -she takes her turn excitedly- The pockets of the suit are bottomless, like Mary Poppins’ handbag, but in order not to lose it, you need to put your hand in with the object, say its name and drop it. To retrieve it, the same thing, put your hand in, say the name and it will appear in your hand. For example, reel one, hypnotic box, etc. Don’t repeat any names so that the objects don’t overlap -she winks at them.

― Nél: Regarding the music box, use these inhibitors to avoid being hypnotised when listening to the melodies -she hands them earplugs with a metallic finish-, they let all sound through except the one from the music box, it gets activated when it is automatically inserted into the ear. Finally, always travel in crowds, avoid shady areas, don’t trust anyone and walk fast but stealthily.

― Rol: That’s all, any questions?

― Láz: Mmm… Yes. And what will you do?

― Nél: We will make sure you are safe, as soon as you cross the door, we will leave the house. We will try to serve as a decoy to the shadows.

― Sal: Mentalists, it’s time to leave, use your skills to keep yourselves alive -Salma pulls a last bit of chalk from an empty case and hastily draws a door, above it it’s shown the direction to open it. She turns the knob and the street appears before them. I’ll drop you off in the Tendetes neighbourhood, the rest on foot, hurry up! -the friends cross the threshold-. May the force be with you -she whispers before closing the door.

Ω

Cars drip down Burjassot’s avenue and people walk in all directions, the terraces of the bars are full.

― Lázaro: Do you know where we are going?

― Nikoleta: I think it’s close by the old Ceramo, by the tram tracks. We continue along the avenue -the boy nods-. Shall we put the dress in the “unnoticed” position? -he nods again.

They start walking. They look from one side to the other, nervously, dodging people until PUUMM! Nikoleta falls to the ground.

― Sir: Are you all right, little girl? -the man and Lázaro help the girl to get up.

― Nik: Yes… Yes, I’m fine -she says, looking at Lázaro in astonishment.

― Láz: Are you all right, Nikoleta?

― Nik: Seriously, I didn’t notice bumping against him or the ground.

― Láz: Let’s get out of here. We can take the parallel route, there aren’t so many people and we’ll get there quicker -she nods.

― Láz: Have you stopped to think about everything that can happen in the next few hours -they walk down a dirty looking street, with noisy people in a couple of bars, the noise of the tram crosses behind them.

― Nik: …mmmh… -says taking a deep breath-, no, and it’s better this way, I guess. It’s just us… Playing to be us -she smiles exaggeratedly.

― Láz: That makes sense. But, we have to be careful, we don’t know what can happen or if they can really help us if we are in danger… -he shrugs.

― Nik: It’s going to be fine.

― Láz: I guess -he says hesitantly-. But if something should happen… Don’t be brave, just run away -Nikoleta frowns, looking at him sceptically.

― Nik: -wraps his arm around his shoulders- In fact, we won’t have to steal and ru-n-a-wa-y. What’s more, do you remember the DVD of Notting Hill signed by Julia Roberts and Hugh Grant that they have in Rosebud Cine? I’m going to buy it.

― Láz: I want the Spice’s t-shirt and a ticket to see Florence and the Machine.

― Nik: It’s a deal -she laughs. She stops in front of a rather gloomy block, with the streetlight flickering intermittently.

― Nik: It’s here, 45 Acacias Street, door 8. Dana Mckully. There’s a light on, should we ring the bell or just open the door?

― Láz: I’ll open the door and you talk upstairs… -the boy smiles-. Give me a hairpin -he reaches out his hand to his friend.

― Nik: Where’s your lock pick? -she asks, looking in his hair.

― Láz: I dropped everything I had in my back pocket, even my chewing gum.

― Nik: It’s from Huelva -the boy opens it and gives it back to her-. You’ve bent it, man, it’s no good, and look at my hair -she tries to straighten it without success-. Here, keep it in case we need it again -the boy takes the hairpin and with a bow lets it drop.

― Láz: Come in, Lady Hairy Rag.

― Nik: Thank you, Lord Dickhead.

They quietly board the lift, get off at the first floor and approach the door.

― Nik: I can’t think of anything convincing to say to them at this hour to take a photo…

― Láz: Wait here a minute -Lázaro returns to the street, a few metres away there are several containers, he looks into the one with the paper and rummages through the debris, picks up a pizza box and goes back upstairs-. That’s it -he hands it to his friend.

― Nik: Hide in the middle of the stairs, pizzas are not delivered with two people -the boy hides-. “Ding dong” -a few steps on the other side, someone peers through the peephole.

― Man: Who is it?

― Nik: Shit!, it’s a man -he whispers-. The pizza you ordered, sir, for Dana Mckully, 45 Acacias Street, door 8.

― Man: We haven’t ordered anything, it must be a mistake.

― Nik: Sir, please, if you don’t take the pizza, I’ll have to pay for it myself -the keys turn and the door opens.

― Man: Hey, girl, I was just about to go to sleep, you must have got the wrong address.

― Nik: Dana Mckully, this is it, right?

― Man: Yes, the information you give me is correct, it’s my wife, but I insist, we haven’t asked for anything, I don’t know why you have this information -someone walks inside the house approaching.

― Dana: Honey, what’s wrong? -a young pregnant woman appears.

― Man: This girl brought us a pizza without us having ordered it and won’t leave without being paid -the woman comes to the door.

― Dana: How much is it? -she says in a thick american accent.

― Nik: Hello, madam, that’ll be €8, please.

― Dana: Guillermo, give her the money and go back to bed -she picks up her purse and looks for the money.

Nikoleta puts the cardboard on the floor and takes out the music box.

― Nik: Dana, Guillermo! -he says to get their attention, the couple turn to look at the girl.

Nikoleta cranks the handle and the music plays. The purse falls to the floor. They stand still. Lázaro, go inside and see if there’s anyone else in the house, the boy rushes in, switching on the lights.

― Láz: There is a small child in the bed -Nikoleta enters and closes the door with her foot.

― Nik: Guillermo, Dana, come with me to your son’s room. Lázaro, the camera. Guillermo, take your son in your arms, get together -the family stands at the girl’s command. Lázaro prepares the camera by placing the film, and nods with a wink when he has it ready-. Now smile and say “ola, q ase?” (hey, watcha doing?)

― Láz: “CLICK” -the boy shoots the picture and after a strong flash, the people disappear-. Xé què loco! (This is crazy) They are gone, look -the boy offers the camera to his friend, through the peephole you can see the photo, the three of them are there, still, breathing but still.

― Nik: Holly Molly!… Shall we go on?

They both leave the house and close the door behind them. Lázaro changes the reel and drops it in one of his pockets, naming the reel aloud “reel no. 1”. They walk down the street in silence, still stunned.

― Láz: You hear?

― Nik: Mmm…?

― Láz: Don’t you think it’s pretty quiet here for Marxalenes, I can even hear myself breathing -they’re walking along the edge of Marxalenes park, next to the tram tracks. Nikoleta stops and looks around.

― Nik: There’s something weird, look at the shadows, let’s go! -she pulls her friend, they start walking really fast.

― Láz: Where are we going?! -he asks fearfully.

― Nik: On the other side of the river, by Na Jordana, taxis pass by one after the other, let’s take one.

― Láz: But we’re going to Benimaclet -he says, contritely-. We’ll get on the tram in a moment or we’ll take Ruaya.

― Nik: The tram runs every 20 minutes and I don’t want people -she says, alarmed-, and there are no taxis here, we go down that street, where the school is, and in three blocks we’ll be on the San José bridge. I want to get out of here now -the boy nods without replying.

The feeling continues to grow. When they reach the traffic lights on the other side of the bridge, a taxi stops at the red signal. The boys calls it and get on it. The driver nods his head in greeting without turning around.

― Nik: To the Benimaclet metro station -the taxi starts and moves down the street, at half throttle.

― Láz: -whispers- Nikoleta, I have the impression that we are taking a detour -the taxi doesn’t turn towards the Jardines de Viveros, it continues towards Plaza de Tetuán.

― Nik: -she nods and leans towards the driver- Turn at Puerta del Mar and take us to Benimaclet along the Alameda, if you push a little harder I’ll pay you for the race.

A few seconds of uncertainty. BOOOMM!!!! The car stops violently, the youngster hit the seat.

― Nik: Hey, are you crazy?!!!!

― Láz: Are you all right, Nikoleta? -he says with a disturbed look on his face.

― Nik: -Nikoleta is sitting up, holding on to the driver’s seat with her head turned towards Lázaro- Yes… well… but I don’t understand. If there’s no one in the street, why did you stop like that? -she angrily shakes the driver’s seat. A few seconds with no answer, the car accelerates in reverse-. HEY!! HEYYYY!!! What are you doing? -she shouts, hitting the driver’s shoulder, looking back and forth.

― Láz: NIKOLETA! LOOOK! -he says, pressing his face against the glass, completely astonished, a horde of humanoid shadows run towards them sliding down the facades-. OOOOOOHHH!!! WE’VE BEEN CATCHED!!!! -he shouts, trying to open the door.

― Nik: NOOOOOO! WE ARE GOING TO DIEEEE!!!! -she shouts pointing towards a car coming at them perpendicularly from across the street.

BOOOOOMMM!!!!! It crashes sideways onto the driver, who is blown to ashes, the airbags deploy, the two cars are stuck together. One on top of the other. Roland quickly gets out of the car, breaks the window with a crowbar and pulls them out of the taxi.

― Rol: Come on, kids, get out of the car! -they do so in a frenzy-. WAKE UP, the suit has protected you! -he shouts as he pulls them along in a hurry-. Put on these backpacks -he hands them a pair of backpacks and a pair of cartridges resembling a mini-cassette as the three of them advance, still in shock-. Insert the cartridge into the reader on the left sleeve of the suit. Press the large button on the left sleeve to open the compartment, with the A side facing you and the arrow pointing down…

― Láz: I can’t!!

― Nik: Give it to me! -she takes him by the hand, opens the hatch and inserts the cartridge without stopping from running-. NOW!

― Rol: Press the small button now!!! ACTIVATE IT!

― Láz: WWWWWOOOOOOOWWWWW!!! -he shouts as his running speed increases.

― Nik: What now?

― Rol: You can now run at a maximum of 80 km/h. Arrive at the station, just as you enter, load travel mode, it’s the B side of the cassette. Don’t take off your backpacks, you need them for the travel mode to work, RUN!

The friends nod and continue running at full speed down Colon street, leaving the man behind. Roland runs after them slowing down, just in front of the shadows, he takes a music box out of his pocket and plays it, the music slowly stops the acolytes, their movements slow down as well as their running, Lázaro and Nikoleta have already disappeared in the distance.

― Rol: Noooooo!!!!! -he shouts as he sees a pair of shadows running after the young couple. SHIT! LEAVE THEM ALONE! -Roland accelerates but the shadows that have escaped are already too far away to be affected by the waves, he slows down and continues to slow the shadows behind him-. IF YOU DO ANYTHING TO THEM I SWEAR YOU WILL PAY, MÉRIDA!!!!

Lázaro and Nikoleta run to Xàtiva station, they have managed to mislead the shadows, they run so fast that only their trail can be seen. They enter the subway.

― Nik: The train, can you hear it? It’s coming in, IT’S OURS!

― Láz: Whatever, we can run!!!
