― Nik: NO!!! Look at the energy marker -a red light flashes.

They hurry down the stairs. Nikoleta looks back, seeing the shadows behind them in the hallway mirrors.

― Nik: Lázaro, we’re being followed! Change the cassette and press travel mode!

They both press it at the same moment they step onto the platform, two large suitcases fall to the ground. With the inertia they cross the doors entering the train stopping next to a lady loaded with more luggage; the shadows appear and get on the train, no one can see them, they search, they trace every corner of the train getting lost in the last car, after inspecting the train the shadows vanish. Nikoleta and Lázaro are still hidden, in a couple more stops they reach a tunnel that leaves the cabin in darkness for a few seconds, the light returns and they appear seated in front of two young men.

Ω

― Roland: Nélida, CAN YOU HEAR ME? -he shouts through the communicator in his ear. Roland walks backwards, in front of him there’s a crowd of shadows that advance very slowly along facades, terraces and asphalt-. I’m arriving at Plaza de América.

― Nélida: Location, Roland!!! TELL ME WHERE ARE YOU? -Nélida is in a van driven at full speed by her sister. She looks through rudimentary, oversized binoculars. They are driving along the right side of the river going towards the sea.

― Rol: I’m in Sorní street with América square, about to cross Puente del Mar! They are on top of me!!!!

― Nél: I’m seconds away! LOCATED! -she says as she sees Roland appear in the square-. Wind up the box and drop it in 5, 4, 3, 2, 1…

Salma speeds past, slamming on the brakes CRRRRIIIIIIIII!!! she skids past Roland, Nélida opens the door and the man jumps inside with the car already starting. He manages to sit down and close the door, the van accelerates. Just as the shadows are about to get to them, the box falls to the ground and continues to beep for a few seconds until the crank stops turning, leaving the hosts still for a moment, seeming inert. The crank stops “Click!”, the wood of the box creaks, the shadows regain their mobility crushing it in their path, at full speed they dissipate through the streets looking for their victims. Salma drives along the pavement until she gives her pursuers the slip over Puente del Regne bridge, under the gaze of the bronze gargoyles, the van disappears up the street into the Cabanyal neighbourhood.

― Rol: I’m old! One more second and I would’ve suffocate.

― Nél: Calm down, Roland, breathe -the van is stopped in a field full of cars. Nélida offers her husband a bottle of water. The man drinks and catches his breath. The woman takes another bottle out of her backpack and offers it to her sister. Salma smiles excitedly.

― Salma: No, thanks… I feel better than ever, it’s been the most exciting thing we’ve experienced in 29 years -she turns and looks at them vehemently-. They’re going to make it, we’re going to save Jimena and Alejo!

Ω

A Metro with a Stop in the Highlands

The subway reviewer goes forward asking for tickets among the commuters.

― Reviewer: Ticket, please. Ticket, please -he reaches out to them-. Gentlemen -a couple of casual-looking guys in their early thirties are in front of them, in seats for four passengers. The youngsters show their tickets-. Lads, please, your tickets.

― Nikoleta: Mmm… One moment please, I have to look for them, we ran down not to miss the train and I don’t know where I put them -the girl searches through her clothes-. Lázaro, did I give them to you or did I keep them?

― Lázaro: I don’t know, we were going so fast so as not to miss the train -he says, digging into his pockets-, that it wouldn’t be strange if they fell off.

― Reviewer: Young people, if you got on without paying, let’s not waste time, say so, you’re not the first nor the last ones to do so. We get off at the next stop and you’ll walk away with a fine.

― Láz: No, we really bought the tickets, I just don’t know where they are -he empties his pockets. One of the boys intervenes.

― Man 1: Excuse me, sir, it’s true what they say, I’ve seen them get on at full speed and enter the train, they had their ticket in their hand when they entered, they must have save them and they just don’t know where.

― Reviewer: Gentleman, I’m sure that’s true, but it’s my duty to check everyone’s tickets, nobody can travel without them. Young man, young lady, please follow me -Nikoleta, in a hurry, bends down and picks up a piece of advertising paper from the floor.

― Nik: Wait, I have the tickets here! -the reviewer turns around and looks at them quizzically, Lázaro observes-. Look, you see, the two tickets I couldn’t find -she says, staring at the worker without blinking. The tickets are nothing more than a pair of Barraca disco leaflet, she holds them in her trembling hand.

― Reviewer: Yes, that’s right, sorry for the inconvenience -the reviewer continues his work, leaving them behind.

― Láz: Since when can you do that? -he exclaims in a whisper, visibly excited. The friends look at the couple with curiosity.

― Nik: It was an impulse, I didn’t know either… -the man in front of her looks at her with amusement, his smile reaches from ear to ear and his laughing eyes are a fine line, infecting Nikoleta who can’t hide her smile even though she tries to contain it by biting her lip.

― Man 2: -the man leans forward, still smiling and looking at Nikoleta- Impressive -he says in a whisper that doesn’t hide his strong accent-. Like Patrick in The Mentalist… mentalist… -he says to himself. Nikoleta and Lázaro look at each other in dismay. The other boy puts his hand on his companion’s shoulder.

― Man 1: Spark, leave them alone -he nods apologetically-. Great trick, lass -he says, looking directly at Nikoleta, with intense green eyes. The man leans back and continues in silence, looking out of the window.

Spark shrugs, pushes his glasses up on the bridge of his nose and returns to his thoughts. The children let out their breath and settle back into their seats.

― Láz: Are you all right, Nikoleta?

― Nik: Yes, a little bit accelerated -her eyes are wide open and watchful.

― Láz: There are still 14 stops left, we have more than an hour, you can sleep if you want and I’ll watch out -the girl leans her head against the window.

― Nik: Don’t worry, I won’t fall asleep.

The girl stares at the pair of boys, the one on the left is Spark, slim, wearing jeans and a waistcoat, with an imposing nose, light blue eyes behind aviator-style glasses, hairless, misaligned teeth, a harelip, the sides of his temples shaved and his red hair pulled back in a bun. He wears two earrings in each ear and has tattoos and several rings on his hand. The other has coppery, frayed hair, green eyes, a thick beard that slightly reveals a thick lower lip. He wears a beret with a green feather twisted on his head, jeans and a sweatshirt with the sleeves torn off that reads “I <3 Anime”, on his hands he wears several rings as well. After the review of the boys, Nikoleta drops her head back on the backrest, gazing up at the ceiling, her lips moving, mumbling her thoughts. Lázaro makes himself comfortable putting his feet up on the seat and hugging his knees, wearily looks out of the window and with a faint whistle he hums from time to time the song from the music box that has been played. One after the other, they leave behind them the different municipalities that surround the great capital.

― Man 2: Hey! Hey, boy! -he says, tapping him on the knee with the book he is reading-. Your stop is next -Lázaro comes out of his enthrallment.

― Láz: …Oh, yes, yes… -he says, looking at the map above the access door-. Thank you.

― Man 1: We go down in Museros. I loved your friend’s trick. I’d like to see it again -he says, putting a card in the boy’s hand.

He approaches the other man who is already waiting at the door and when the train stops, they get off. Lázaro looks at the men while the train continues its journey. He reads the card: “Spark MacCowan & Deer Cunningham, Private detectives from the Highlands, a particular way to find out the truth”. The voice over the public address announces the arrival at the next stop. Nikoleta glances at the card.

― Nik: Detectives?

― Láz: Looks like it -he says with an excited smile, as if he’s just seen something he’s never seen before. It’s cool.

― Nik: We get off on this one.

― Láz: Yep -they both get off the train, no one else does, there is no one at the stop, they cross the tracks towards the station building-. I don’t dare to check Google Maps in case we’re being monitored.

Nikoleta takes her phone out of her pocket and takes Lázaro’s phone, walks over to a rubbish truck that is heading towards a dumpster and throws them in.

― Nik: One less problem.

― Láz: Okay, we don’t have a mobile phone, so how do you plan to continue?

― Nik: Walking and without a phone, we don’t need it. A few years ago I came here a couple of times, we won’t get lost, the house we are looking for is far away, but it is parallel to this street.

They both start walking, they don’t say anything, everyone is asleep, it’s a quiet village, with many low houses, every now and then they pass someone walking the dog or wandering in the fresh air.

They walk a few streets until they reach the number they are looking for and then they stop at the gate.

― Nik: Fuck Duck… There is not a single doorbell with a name and there are 16 numbers. Where do you think our Ekaitz Aramburu Berenguer lives -a voice startles them, a woman appears in the doorway, pulled by 2 dogs that sniff the youngsters.

― Woman: Ekaitz lives in 8B, but he is not at home now -turns the key and opens the door.

― Nik: I don’t know who he is, I’ve made an online appointment with him to buy some skates and he doesn’t answer my WhatsApp.

― Woman: Skates at this hour? -she looks at her in total disbelief-. You should be at your house and not wandering around -she grumbles under her breath as she opens the door, just before closing it, she says-, he works at the vet clinic in Llaurador Valencià street. That poor creature works harder than a donkey, and that’s no good, you get old sooner.

― Nik: OK! -turns her head looking towards where the clinic might be-. Thank you…

― Woman: Okay, okay, okay… Bona nit (good night) -the woman interrupts her and closes the door.

― Nik: -closes her eyes and clenches her jaw, releasing all the air through her nose with her fists clenched-. We have to go in that direction.

― Láz: Well?

― Nik: -whispers to the boy in a rough voice, pounding her fists- “Cau la nit, no convé abusar” (it’s getting dark, don’t ask for too much), she’s been very helpful, it is what it is.

― Láz: Go on, I’ll follow you -he says, still laughing.

Nikoleta and Lázaro walk under the yellow light of the street lamps, everything is quiet, the crickets can be heard and a cool breeze is blowing. They continue in a straight line to almost the end of the village, where a residential area is being built, in front of a wide promenade there is a sign with a dog and a cat on it. There is no light, everything is switched off, but the shutter is half up and the door is ajar.

― Láz: Nikoleta, we need an animal to go in there, don’t we? -she stops and looks at him.

― Nik: You think so?

― Láz: We can go and take a picture of him when he shows up, but in case that doesn’t turn out well, maybe we should pretend with something. Like we did with the pizza box and the leaflet.

― Nik: I could try to make him believe that a plastic bottle is a cat, maybe it’ll work.

― Láz: A bottle, això no pot ser! (No way!) It’s going to look weird -he says, shaking his head.

― Nik: I’m a lucky girl, I’m sure we’ll find something useful in a container somewhere -Lázaro laughs-. Let’s go and look.

― Láz: Ughhh… I don’t know.

― Nik: Look over there, on the other side, where this part of the walk ends, there are five containers. Look for something inside those, OK? I’ll look in these ones in the corner. You trust me -Lázaro walks towards them. They both rummage through the rubbish.

― Láz: Nikoleta! -he shouts with half of his body inside the container. She looks around for him-. I’ve got it!!!! -Lázaro pulls a dirty, broken stuffed animal out of the bin. A kind of bear with a blue face, the rest of its hair red, with a hole in one eye, a dirty green crest, and its arms are more than half a metre long, a piece of chain hanging from its hand, wadding coming out of its ears, and some wires hanging down its back. She comes closer, quickening her pace at the sight of him-. What do you think?

― Nik: Ha ha ha ha -she laughs in disbelief-. I knew it, it was here just for us -she picks it up with a certain amount of disgust and lifts it up-. It has a label, let me read it “Los Temblors, Juguetes Vir, Made in Valencia, Spain” (the tremblers, toys Vir). What do you think of the name Ximo? -she exaggerates a valencian accent.

― Láz: Would you really do that for me? Ximo, benvingut a la familia! (welcome to the family) -he says with an excited gesture. He picks up the toy and kisses it on the neck. She looks at him gingerly.

― Nik: We’re gonna make it. Let’s go -he nods and they head for the clinic’s door. Lázaro drags the toy, it’s wet, and leaves dripping on the pavement-. Shhh! Take Ximo in your arms, he’s not dead yet.

― Láz: Ximo is so disgusting -he says with a crooked smile-, he doesn’t want to live anymore, he smells of fish and olive liquid, take him in YOUR arms!

― Nik: I can’t, I have to concentrate, and it smells really bad. Take it, I’m going to ring the bell -Lázaro picks him up in his arms and they both approach the shutter with a serious look on their faces.

ZZZZZZZ!!! The doorbell rings. It takes a moment but they can hear some noise coming from the inside, the light comes on. The door opens and, crouching down, a twenty-something, bearded, half-asleep-eyed guy peers out from under the shutter.

― Vet: Hello, good evening, how can I help you?

― Nik: Good evening, I’m sorry to bother you, it’s about my dog Ximo -she looks sadly at the toy-. I don’t know what’s wrong with him, but he’s vomiting non-stop, he can’t stand up. That’s why my brother is carrying him in his arms -she wraps her arm around Lázaro’s shoulders and they both look at the vet with a pout.

― Vet: Sure… -he looks at the cuddly toy in bewilderment-. Wait just one second -the boy pulls the shutter and it goes all the way up, more lights come on and he goes over to open the door-. Please come in. Sorry, you’ve caught me watching a film and almost half asleep, we don’t have 24-hour service, but we’ve got two very sick dogs and I didn’t want them to be left alone.

Let’s take a look at Ximo. Come into the consulting room, please - the three of them enter the room and Lázaro carefully places the cuddly toy on the observation table. The vet looks at him, his face is unhinged, as if something doesn’t quite convince him-, and what do you say is wrong with him?

― Nik: Well, he had diarrhoea a while ago, he vomited on the way here, and he walks like he’s drunk.

― Vet: Wow… have you given him something to eat or do you know if he has access to any unusual food?

― Nik: Mmmm… no, not that I know of.

― Láz: -he sees her friend hesitating for a moment-. Yes, well, actually I think he ate a box of chocolates that we had in the room. When I got there it was empty, I didn’t eat them, and my sister doesn’t like chocolate.

― Vet: Wow… Chocolate, that’s quite harmful for a dog, but for him to be in this condition he must have eaten a lot, because he looks very poorly -he takes him in his hands and auscultates him, looks at his pupils with the torch, observes his throat and finally takes his temperature, “Pi Pi” sounds the thermometer-. Wow… no temperature. I’m a bit worried, I don’t know what to tell you about Ximo, I think I should do an x-ray and start an IV. Pick him up, please, and we’ll take him to the next room.

Lázaro carefully picks up the stuffed toy and follows the vet. He looks at Nikoleta and winks at her with a smile, she returns the gesture with a thumbs-up.

― Nik: What is your name?

― Vet: My name is Ekaitz. Yours?

― Nik: Lázaro and Nikoleta.

― Ekaitz: OK. Lázaro, leave Ximo lying over his face that way, there’s no need to stay by his side, he’s so weak he won’t move -he says as he places the equipment-. Now we’re going to leave the room while I shoot the rays -“PIIUURGG”, a mechanical noise-. That’s it -they step into the room and the image comes on the screen, a cloud of wadding and gnawed fabric. The boy stares at it with his mouth half open, running his hand over his face and rubbing his eyes.

― Nik: You look worried, do you think he’ll recover? -she asks, holding her nerve.

― Eka: Wow… I’m not really sure. How old is Ximo?

― Nik: We adopted him when he was 9 years old, and we’ve had him for 7 years.

― Eka: …Sure… that explains everything… He’s really old, I think that’s why he’s in such a bad form. What I’m going to do is put in an IV and give him an envelope to purge him, so that he can expel all the chocolate that’s left inside. Is that OK?

― Nik: Yes, of course. Do whatever it takes to get him back on his feet, please -Lázaro nods.

The vet prepares a dropper, sticks the IV in his paw. He prepares a pipette and empties it into his mouth, the liquid drips down his plush tongue. The boy picks him up in his arms, takes the dropper and carries the stuffed animal to another room, where there are several animals in their cages, all asleep, badly injured. He carefully puts Ximo down in a cage, closes it, turns off the light and goes out.

― Eka: Well, that’s it, leave it here tonight, and since I’m here, I’ll take a look at it so you can have some rest. If anything happens, I’ll call you… -the boy touches his chin without getting over his astonishment, his face suddenly wrinkles as he smells the stench rising from his hands.

― Nik: You’ll need our details, won’t you?

― Eka: Right -he says, turning his attention back to the boys still in disgust, pulling out a sheet to fill in from behind the counter-. The consultation is 35 euros, but since it’s an emergency and at night, I don’t know what to tell you. We don’t usually provide this service. So I’ll tell my boss about it, and tomorrow when we call you to tell you how Ximo’s doing, you come and let her tell you the price, OK?

― Nik: Sure. Tomorrow, no problem, we’ll come by at midday to see how Ximo is and we’ll pay.

― Láz: Please give me some water, I’m very thirsty -Nikoleta looks at her friend.

― Eka: I need water too, and cold -he says, ruminating to herself-. Do you want anything? -he asks the girl-. I think I have some soft drinks.

― Nik: Cold water is fine.

The boy goes down the corridor and enters another room. Nikoleta and Lázaro come out of the reception, a few steps from the door, into the street.

― Nik: Water?

― Láz: You’re going to traumatise him -he says, wrinkling his face with a grin from ear to ear-. Take the picture now and we’ll be on our way.

Lázaro takes the camera out of his pocket and prepares himself. The vet arrives with two small bottles of water and a glass. He gives the bottles to each of them.

― Eka: Here, for you -the vet sees the camera in Lázaro’s hand-. Wow, my parents had one of those, I think the last time they took a picture of me with it, I wasn’t even an inch tall. It surprises me is that you have one.

― Láz: With this one we take photos with Ximo. We like old cameras, they have a different style.

― Eka: Of course, old things have their charm. And as for Ximo, I’m going to do everything I can, but I’d like you to bear in mind that he’s in a very bad form and at his age, an accident like this could be dangerous.

― Nik: It’s just that chocolate drives Ximo absolutely crazy -she laughs at her own amusement.

― Láz: The camera has one photo left, I’ll throw it to you for being so nice to us -the boy thinks about it.

― Eka: Thank you, but it’s not necessary.

― Nik: I insist, come on.

― Eka: Ok, but take me out in one piece, my father always cut off my feet and there was space on top. It made me so angry.

― Láz: Done. Strike a pose and say “ola, q ase” (hey, watcha doing?) -Ekaitz puts the glass down, rubs his face and smiles looking at the camera, says the phrase. Lázaro shoots, the flash blinds everything for a moment. The vet is gone-. We’ve got him. You’ve been top-notch.

― Nik: Thank you -she says as he closes the door of the clinic and pulls the shutter down to the floor-. Let’s go back to the centre, to Plaza Mayor, we have to take a taxi back to Valencia.

― Láz: …a taxi? -he stops-. I don’t want to.

― Nik: The last metro left at 23:28. We left the clinic at 23:31. We missed it. We walk to the taxi rank, and look at the driver’s face, if he has a human face, we get in, et pareix? (how does that sound?) -she asks with a friendly gesture.

― Láz: Mucho vale… (very ok)

― Nik: That doesn’t rhyme.

Ω

Roland pulls a small case out of the boot of a nearby vehicle, it looks like a radio frequency equipment, he is assembling a radio control like the one in toy cars, but it is large, he inserts the joysticks and the antenna. Salma is standing in the door opening, stretching to reach up with an antenna, more than a metre long, the roof of the van, once she does, she runs a cable through the inside of the vehicle, removes the steering wheel, attaches another gadget that fits on the steering column and connects a camera attached to the windscreen. Nélida is standing a few metres away, with binoculars on, watching the streets.

― Salma: This is ready.

― Roland: Checking -he says, moving the axes-. Right?

― Sal: Right - the wheels of the car move in that direction.

― Rol: Left?

― Sal: Correct.

― Rol: Acceleration?

― Sal: Right -the accelerator pedal sinks.

― Rol: Brake?

― Sal: Correct, very correct.

― Rol: Very well. Let’s proceed then -he says, turning on several switches. Salma starts the van by turning the ignition-. Nélida, is the road clear?

― Nélida: Route clear. But you are forgetting the holograms -she says, taking off her glasses and looking at the van.

― Sal: Mmm… okay -she pulls out a couple of gadgets that look like an acetate projector. He tapes them to the dashboard. He turns them on and the figures of Nélida and Roland appear on the seats, but they seem to float and look a bit crooked-. Just a moment and I’ll put them back in place -she peels the tape off slightly, checks that they are now calibrated, and sticks them back on-. Ready -he says, getting out of the van and closing the car doors behind him.

― Rol: Nélida, route clear?

― Nél: Clear.

Roland puts on binoculars even bigger than his wife’s. The van pulls out of the open field and disappears into the streets.

― Sal: How many do you think they will be able to photograph? -she asks standing next to him.

― Rol: Hopefully two or three at least. If something goes wrong, we don’t have the capacity to rescue them again. His tone was a little harsher than he intended.

― Nél: They’re putting their heads on the line and doing a job that doesn’t belong to them. They’ve just found out what they are. The children are doing enough -she says scowling at Roland.

― Rol: I’m sorry, you’re right -he replies without taking his foot off the wheel.

― Sal: If Mérida dies, there will be moves on the board. Maybe we should also consider what the antediluvians and the ravens will do.

― Nél: Maybe it’s better for us to live -she says, sighing and looking up, pondering this thought. She lowers her binoculars and looks at his sister.

― Rol: How? -his expression changes after the two women’s words. He stops the radio-controlled vehicle at the side of the road, lifts up his binoculars and looks at them in frustration-. No, that won’t happen. After Mérida dies, which she will, the Tinddaya will be free again. And the necromancers will be so tightly controlled, they won’t move a hair without us knowing about it.

― Nél: Of course -she approaches her husband, nods and strokes his chin to change his angry expression-. Roland, that option is also on our minds. We have to get out of here, get in, we’re leaving. Salma, you drive -she nods.

Roland takes the passenger seat, puts on his binoculars and continues to drive the van. Nélida follows behind. Salma starts the car and they leave the open field.

― Sal: It’s not going to be that easy, but between dying or trying, I’d rather try.

Ω

They both walk towards Plaza Mayor with haste, without saying a word, concentrating on their objective. Some footsteps are heard behind them, they have been hearing them at the same distance for a few minutes now, Lázaro turns around, observes, he sees no one. He looks ahead and continues walking. The footsteps are heard behind them again, he turns again, there is no one.

― Láz: Nikoleta…

― Nik: Mmm? -she mumbles without coming out of her thoughts.

― Láz: Can you hear the footsteps?

― Nik: No, what steps? -her body tenses up and she walks faster.

― Láz: They’re following us, I’ve turned around a couple of times but I don’t see them -Lázaro pauses, squinting to try to focus on something in the distance.

There is a faint murmur of the city, some cars passing by, someone on a balcony, someone walking in the distance, but no one can be heard or seen nearby. Nikoleta is a few metres away from him, she turns impatiently and moves towards him, taking him by the shoulders, urging him to walk.

― Nik: We have to keep moving, Lázaro -she squeezes the boy’s shoulders in a reassuring gesture. The footsteps are no longer heard.

In just 3 minutes they reach Calle Mayor (main street), they walk towards Valencia’s direction, pass cars and a few people.

― Nik: Fuck! -she says, rushing. Angrily.

― Láz: What is it?

― Nik: There are all those taxis in Valencia. So many metro and EMT (city transport company) and no public transport at this time of the day… Pussy Lucy!

― Láz: We can go into the kebap and borrow a mobile phone to call a taxi -he suggests, looking at the shop that is still open. The girl takes a deep breath.

― Nik: No, no phones… -sentences more abrupt than intended, she turns to the boy and looks at him, he approaches her and lightly strokes her forearm.

― Láz: What do you propose? -he closes his eyes for a moment, dropping his head forward.

― Nik: We continue walking, this avenue leads us to Valencia and links several villages, sooner or later we will come across a taxi.

― Láz: Freeze! -he looks up and over Nikoleta’s shoulder he sees two figures standing firmly on the pavement that is in front, watching them from a distance-. They’re there, the ones following us -he mumbles in discomfort, she turns her head slowly and watches them, not knowing what to do for a moment.

The figures are between the beams of light from the street lamps, they are dark enough not to see their faces, not to know if they are shadows.

― Nik: Come on -she says, squeezing his friend’s hand and pulling him along.

― Láz: Wait -he says, seeking his pursuers’ gaze, lets go of her friend’s hand and takes a step forward. He observes, something pushes him to whistle, he hits the first two notes of the melody of the music box-. “Fiii, Fuuu…”

“Fii, Fuuu, fiiiiiiii, fu, fu, fu, fu, fuuuuuuuu” replies one of the figures.

― Láz: Fotre, quin susto!! (Fuck, what a fright) -he mumbles between his teeth as he rushes relieved to cross the street.

― Nik: Where are you going? -pulls him but doesn’t stop him.

― Láz: Why are you like this? -he approaches angrily, giving one of them a shove. As he moves, the light hits their faces.

― Spark: You do the talking.

― Deer: Me? -he says, pointing to his chest-. It was yourself that wanted to follow them and never say a word.

― Spa: We’ve had this out already -he says, turning his face and looking away.

― Dee: Erm… we’re following you for we know what you are, and we’ve a notion you’re caught up in something right serious -his tone is conciliatory but he can’t help sounding concerned.

― Nik: Oh yeah… And what are we? -she asks expectantly.

― Spa: Mentalists, Teraghe`n type 2 or 3, the pair of you. Yon disturbance this morning was the Empress. There were shadow trackers below, we felt them, they were after something, and then you appeared. You’re on to something big -he points a finger at them, which is immediately lowered by Deer.

― Dee: That’s why we’re following you, and that’s the whole of it -the friends gasp, not knowing what to say, their nerves dissipating and giving way to an exhausted look-. Here… easy now, we’re for helping you, whatever it is. But you’ll have to tell us what you’ve done to turn Valencia on its head. Aye?

― Láz: Nope -he says dryly.

― Spa: Dè? (what?) -he exclaims in a sigh, raising his eyebrows.

― Láz: You first.

― Dee: -looks at his partner, who nods after a second’s thought, looks back at him and raises his head in invitation- Right enough… first things first -raises his hand palm up, Spark does the same-. “Tihulan, Tinddaya, ayt Naga” -he murmurs and a drawing appears illuminated under the skin of both boys. Nikoleta and Lázaro take a deep breath, relieved-. Spark’s the sensitive, I’m the mentalist. Private detectives, the two of us, making a living off what we’ve got. That’s the ordinary half of it. But what makes us the only ones of our kind is that Spark’s a great-great-grandson of Sherlock Holmes and myself a great-great-nephew of John Watson, and we work with every last object the two of them turned up in their adventures, which helps no end -the youngsters return a kind but puzzled look-. Do those names not ring a bell with you?

― Láz: Not really…like at all -the girl twists her face, not knowing who they are.

― Dee: Right, it’s yourselves now. What have you done?

Lázaro begins to narrate the last hours under the gazing eyes of the two detectives until the present moment. Deer curls a lock of his beard and Spark has his hands under his nose in a triangle.

― Spa: Can I see the watch? -Lázaro opens his fanny pack, the light from the street illuminates the watch’s fob-. A Dhia… (dear God)

― Dee: Would you look at that… There’s no time to be losing, you’ll have to away back to Valencia and get Tesla out. Of the whole list they gave you she’s the strongest; yon woman is a marvel.

― Spa: She’s in the Joaquín Sorolla Hotel, in Beniferri, next the Palacio de Congresos. TED conferences the whole weekend -he looks at his mobile for a few seconds-. She’s on first thing in the morning -he says with a stern look on his face.

― Dee: No taxi, no bike, no metro; that would be daft -he says, taking two capsules out of his pocket, he presses a button, drops them and behind a cloud of smoke two scooters appear-. Battery enough in them to reach Jaén, they’ll do a hundred, and they work like any other. Have you ridden one before? -they both nod-. Aye, there’s nothing to them, brake and accelerate -he says with a wave of his arms, offering the scooters to the boys.

― Nik: And you, aren’t you coming?

― Spa: No. On your own you’re the more use and the harder to follow. We carry the ring -he says gruffly.

― Dee: We’ve learned to fool it, mind -he whispers, winking at them-, though not the whole way. We cannot come with you; you’re better on your own. We’ll keep an eye, and we’ll step in if it turns ugly. Aye?

― Nik: Okay Dokay -she replies-, taking in the situation.

― Láz: M’apanya (That’ll do me) -he replies back with a half-smile.

― Spa: Up you get. Forty-five minutes you have to get there. Use what you’ve got to reach Tesla -Nikoleta and Lázaro get on the scooters, switch them on.

― Dee: It’ll be fine, the both of you. Away you go now -he gives them a smiling, convinced face.

They both push the scooter and it speeds off, lost in the distance of Calle Mayor. Spark and Deer walk in the opposite direction, getting lost in the narrow streets.

Ω

Three middle-aged men are together in a hotel room on what looks like a high-rise floor, the wind is blowing strongly. The windows are open, one of them is sitting on the chair on the balcony, the other one is leaning on the glass panel and the last one is walking around the room. You can see the sea, the castle of Santa Barbara and the whole city of Alicante lit up by the night lights. The music comes from the port.

― Man 1: NO, NO, NO, FUCK! When the fuck are they going to die! -he exclaims, looking at a black orb he holds in his hand, he throws it against the bed, it’s so heavy that it sinks the mattress-. They know where the Empress is, they know.

― Man 2: Calm down, Radon -says the one sitting on the balcony’s chair with a serene gesture-, I’ve ordered increased surveillance at the portals. We have the entire wave spectrum of the dark plane under control. If they show up, we’ll know where.

― Radon: Don’t fuck with me, Mercury! Those motherfuckers are always one step ahead of us! We’re waiting for them to open up a sand gorge to find out where our target is hiding, it’s TOO RISKY!!!! Those old men have no power in the present, but their trickery leaves us in our panties -he says, kicking the pouffe and snorting with rage.

― Mercury: Not this time, you can be sure of that. Generating an anachronism on the plane of shadows does not allow the movement of living beings. Only shadows will come, and we are the masters of darkness in our time.

― Rad: Fuck, it’s like betting everything on one card. We don’t know what’s going to cross that throat -he says, leaving the room and slamming the door “PUUUUMMM”.

― Man 3: Should we be this calm? The situation is getting out of control and we haven’t planned anything beyond what Radon sets out.

― Mer: Arsenic, they have no chance of taking Mérida alive, she’ll die if she crosses the plane -he finishes, taking a swig of rum cowboy and draining it in one gulp, he stands up-. They’ll open the portal, but they won’t be able to cross.

― Arsenic: I know that, and that doesn’t worry me. But even without being able to cross the two timelines, you know they’re up to something we can’t even imagine. If Mérida falls, they will be there and not just as observers, they will intervene and it’ll be no small thing. I am uneasy, I feel the Teraghe`n in an accelerated state.

― Mer: -shook his head- The Empress will be ours and the Antediluvians will be nothing but a joke. That is how the story goes, there is no other way for us Ravens -he sighs deeply, turns his head to look at his brother and lets the air out suddenly-. Let’s go get her.

― Ars: Come on -he nods with an evil half-smile.

Mercury crosses inside. He takes the orb from the bed and manipulates it, a beam of black light floods the room, a wide open space of darkness rises, he continues to manipulate the orb and it begins to levitate in front of him. He raises his hands with open palms and pronounces a few words in a guttural voice and that seem to steal his breath “Kaixo, Bele laguna! Dei egiten dizut!” (Greetings, my raven friend, I summon you), out of the mists appears what looks like a cloak and a raven mask, all in black. He approaches the garment, and it, as if it was alive, unfurls over its wearer. Arsenicus approaches, utters the same words, and a leather garment replete with buckles and a pair of spectacles alights upon him. Mercury claps his hands twice and the darkness disappears.

Radon enters the room in a hurry, as if something has called him and he knows he must return, his brothers are on the balcony with the raven’s garment on them.

― Rad: Go for it, right?

― Mer: So be it -he replies.

― Rad: Are you going to summon the shadows of the river Andarax? -the other nods-. Be it.

― Ars: Hurry up, we’ll wait for you in Los Millares. The Teraghe`n is very restless at the moment, it won’t be tomorrow. Be it.

The two crows open their wings, disappearing into a thick black mist that shoots towards the horizon, leaving behind a sound similar to a chorus of guttural, sickly voices.

Radon takes the orb lying on the bed and proceeds as his brothers have done. The clothing appears in the darkness, an aviator jacket, a helmet and a scarf covering his jaw. The darkness disappears. He picks up the orb, pushes it against his chest, absorbing it. He runs to the balcony, spreads his wings and disappears from the hotel.

Ω

There is a heavy silence, as if life had departed, but it is not a natural silence, not even a silence befitting the rooms of the Empress’s palace. It is an absence that serves as a preamble to what is about to happen. The Teraghe`n shifts in a way never before appreciated by any Tinddaya.

― Alejo: Jimena… Jimena… Can you hear me? -he whispers, cornered in the shadow. There is not a single drop of light in the rocky room, nothing, Alejo lies on the floor of the cell-. Jimena, I can feel it -he says helplessly, raging-, the Teraghe`n is fluctuating in the negative. It’s the crows, they’re using the Necropolis of Los Millares to summon the dead and get to the palace… -he sighs exhausted, the darkness is absorbing his energy as he tries to use the Teraghe`n-. But there is something else… BASTARDS! -he punches the stone-. The Antediluvians are powerful even from the past. Bummer! They know Mérida may die and they will fight to take over the shadow empire. We are too weak to fight against the Antediluvians and the Ravens. We have to get out of here, Jimena… -Alejo leaves himself completely exhausted.

Ω

Somewhere lost in Macaronesia.

Footsteps sound hurried and clumsy on the tunnels’ rocks, the light of the torches illuminates his twisted face as he passes by them, the door opens, he pauses for a moment, no one gives him entrance, he advances through the room, it is dark, gloomily lit by dense beams of light emanating from red candles floating in the air. There’s a stooped man figure dressed in a threadbare robe, wearing leather sandals, his face is covered by a hood and his only blue-eyed, almost blind pupils are visible, his hands are black, dripping with a black liquid similar to pitch. He advances to the presbytery with his head down, stops to kneel in front of the altar, and emits a sound that intermingles hissing with speech.

Just in front of the huge red shield that crowns the altar and on which are drawn the lines of the necromancer’s lineage, there are three large vessels, they are like deformed bathtubs, you can hear the bubbles and some splashing. There is an apparently human figure in each of the vessels, only the nostrils are showing through which you can hear a metallic breathing, the liquid ranges from blood red to coal black, it is dense.

― Man: Tamaraggwa, Wayota… (good morning, demon) -says the man in a broken, old, gangly voice.

The figures move under the liquid at the sound of the fayac’s (priest’s) voice, their decrepit heads peep out, their skulls covered with skin, the liquid dripping down, blurring their deformed and aged figures. Their sockets are hollowed out and their eyes are completely whitish, blind, protruding from their sockets, their jaws drooping, their teeth barely held in place by receding gums, their noses and ears hanging disproportionately.

― Wayota 1: Speak, fayac -the voice is hoarse and distant and a red glow grows in the Antediluvian’s eyes.

― Fayac: Wayota, be merciful to allow my presence, I beg your undeserved attention, for the news I carry -he pauses, there is no response, and continues-. The necromancer of the legendary triad. Something has happened that could lead to her death at the earliest opportunity. There are bloodlines in motion to overthrow the Empress. She is getting weaker and weaker, this is a unique opportunity to seize her essence and take positions in the future. At the same time, the ravens are focusing all their power on guarding the shadows’ plane. They have summoned the genius of Andarax, they intend to take the Empress’ place, and also to prevent us from making any manoeuvres.

― Wayota 1: -he takes a deep breath and one of his teeth falls into the liquid, it seems to sink but slowly rises up his neck until it settles back into his gum- We have sensed her presence, her anger is so great that it travels back into the past, to us, but she loses power, her being weakens -he pauses to think-. Do whatever it takes to prevent the Empress from regaining her strength, turn her into essence and bring her to this time of ours. Destroy any of our descendants if they oppose our will, without mercy, the rest of Tinddaya are irrelevant.

― Fayac: I beg your pardon if I ask you, my lords, if you have any inclination to proceed in this matter.

― Wayota 1: Send out the Blind Hunters, revive them with the blood of the 10 kings, their power must resist the throat of sand.

― Fayac: So be it -they don’t answer and their skulls sink back into the liquid.

The priest leaves the room, walks down the increasingly dark passages until he enters a narrow corridor that leads to a single rickety, leaky door. He opens it, everything is dusty and damp. His eyes peer into the darkness, the room is dug into the rock, it is empty, he looks up, three wooden mannequins hang from the ceiling, they are tied with chains around their necks, hands, trunk and feet. He observes them for a few seconds in suspicion, they are inert creatures created by the Wayota themselves, but their thirst for blood makes them break orders, the Fayac fears that they could kill the Empress.

He waves his hands and a black circle full of runes is drawn in the air, the circle turns into a black figure, featureless, formless, an acolyte at the priest’s command.

― Fayac: Bring them down without breaking the chains that bind them -the shadow ascends the wall, undoes the knots in the strings that hold them, and one by one lays them on the ground-. Follow me and fetch them -the shadow picks up the three puppets and advances after the priest.

He enters a spacious room, lit by pitch lamps, the walls are covered with shelves full of labelled jars, there are large wooden tables, they have dismembered bodies on them. There are several men working in the room, when they see the priest entering with the mannequins they stop.

― Fayac: Clear a table. We must awaken the Blind Hunters at the will of the Wayota -the men clear a table by throwing everything on it to the floor. The shadow drops the three mannequins on the table-. Give them life with the kings’ blood, of the ten. The hunters must travel to the future with sufficient power.

One of the servants goes to a far corner of the room, where there is a chest with a huge padlock, and takes out three keys with which he opens it, one turn per key. From the inside takes out a tray containing ten vials and approaches the table. The other servants prepare the hunters, on their chests there is a hole just where the heart would be. To each one of the vials they put a nipple, they put three drops in each hole of the puppets. In the first vial the label says “Alulim”, in the following one “Alalgar”, “Enmeluanna”, “Enmegalanna”, “Tammuz”…

The fayac approaches with a candle, and lets the wax drop over the hole, sealing the chest. One by one he binds new chains around their necks, wrists, ankles and waists and as he does so, the metal gives off a pestilential smoke and they close on themselves. The servants withdraw. The priest raises his hands and the rooms’ shadows begin to tremble, a guttural rattle between whistles-. “Agoñe yacoron yñatzahaña chacoñamet” (I swear by the bone of that day when you became great), the figures shake with spasmodic movements. The priest pronounces the words again and the puppets stand on the table.

― Fayac: Come down, you have work to do.

Ω

30 minutes later they are already rolling through Valencia city, they have taken the Germans Machado’s avenue that goes to Benicalap, they continue along the tramway tracks until they almost reach the junction, they turn left, as the low houses change for an endless number of skyscrapers in just one street.

They arrive to the Palacio de Congresos, it is full of printed advertising for the Ted Talks, but the doors are closed and the workers are cleaning and clearing up. There is a cleaner taking out the rubbish.

― Nik: -approaches the woman, Lázaro waits- Excuse me, please, is it over already?

― Madam: Yeh, darlin’, abou’ twen’y minutes back -she answers in a thick Andalusian accent-, yer late.

― Nik: And do you know where the speakers are?

― Madam: Course, my lovely, they’re all shut in over there, in the ’otel Zorolla. Makin’ sure we all know there’s money abou’ -she says, pointing to the hotel just across the street, 20 metres away.

― Nik: They’re going to save the world, I think, I suppose they can afford it.

― Madam: Then let ’em save it from an ’ostel. Ea (Andalusian expression).

― Nik: OK! All the best -she says, walking away.

― Madam: ’Appy to ’elp -she says, punching the bag into the container.

The two of them approach the hotel, the building stands imposing, its light contrasts the dark sky.

― Láz: My ass is sore and my knees are shaking.

― Nik: When we finish taking all the photos, we’ll go to a spa.

― Láz: Now that you say that mention it, what do we do after taking the photo?

― Nik: Find Ampère, hide out until the clock stops, or destroy it if they find us -she says helplessly, raising her eyebrows and biting her lips-. Let’s go inside.

Lázaro presses a button, encapsulates the scooters and puts them in his fanny pack. They both enter the reception area. It is sophisticated, with large windows to the outside, there are armchairs with lamps for reading, but there is no one there, above the reception desk there is a Joaquín Sorolla’s portrait. Behind the counter there is a receptionist who gives them a forced smile when she sees them well-dressed but dishevelled, with dirty hands and faces. They both approach the reception lounge.

― Receptionist: Good evening, how can I help you?

― Nik: Good evening, we are here to see Mrs. Tesla, is she at the hotel at the moment? May we see her?

― Receptionist: I’m afraid not, madam. Once the lectures are over, we cannot give out information about our clients. I am very sorry, you may wish to come to tomorrow’s lecture by Jean Jaques Ampère, which is highly recommended.

― Nik: Ampère!!! is he here too? -he turns to her friend and looks at her with surprise. Nikoleta reads the girl’s name on the badge on her jacket-. Monica, you are so generous with others and you like to help so much that you are going to take us to them, aren’t you?

― Receptionist: That’s right, ma’am, they’re both here. I am here for whatever you need -she says with a smile and a genuine look.

― Nik: Good Goose, take us to Tesla and then help us with Ampère.

― Receptionist: I will be happy pleased to help you. Please follow me -she says, stepping out from the counter-. This way -she points.

Nikoleta looks at his friend and winks. They both follow the receptionist.

― Nik: Thug Life -closes her eyes with satisfaction and pouts.

― Láz: Seeh!! (yeeah!) -crosses his arms over his chest with his fingers in victory.

They both follow her into the restaurant, there are still a few tables in the living room, but there is a small reserved space where about ten people are chatting over drinks. They all stop when they see the receptionist with the young couple behind her.

― Receptionist: Good evening, gentlemen, ladies. I am very sorry to interrupt your evening, these two gentlemen who are with me are looking for Mrs Tesla and Mr Ampère. Thank you for your attention -the receptionist turns around and returns to her place of work, the diners guests stare at Lázaro and Nikoleta in amazement, not understanding the intrusion.

― Man 1: Hello, how can we help you? -he says with some impatience.

― Nik: Good evening, we work for the scientific magazine “Horse and Hound”, it is an University of Valencia’s magazine -the girl takes the Barracas leaflet out of her pocket and shows it to everyone as a way of identification-, as you can see, this is my identification as editor -Ampère leans back with satisfaction.

― Láz: Can we take a photo of them?

― Ampère: A photo? he says with a certain self-conscious air, as if he had half fallen for Lázaro’ trick.

― Tesla: Well, we can start the interview whenever you want, and we’d better start the photos now, we’re tired, it’s been a long day. Where do you want us to stand? -she says, standing next to Ampère.

― Láz: Better standing up, full-length -the scientists nod-. We can go out to the reception, there’s room for everyone.

― Tesla: Whenever you want, we are ready.

― Nik: Lázaro, there is room for 5 people per photo, there are three reels left. We don’t know who the others are, but in case they are important people, shall we photograph them too?

― Láz: Sure, tell them to come -he says with full conviction.

― Nik: Excuse me -he says, approaching the table-. We want to take your picture too, in two groups of five and five, will you come with us, please? -the group of scientists nods, they approach the hall with the others-. Make two groups of five, one on this side and one on the other.

― Láz: Nikoleta!

― Nik: What?

― Láz: They will disappear when the photo is taken, the others will get scared and run away.

― Nik: That’s right. I’ll keep them distracted, take the picture, change the film and shoot again while I’m talking to them, OK?

― Láz: Done -he picks up the camera and looks through the peephole-. Tesla, Ampère and company, your photo in 3, 2, 1 BROOOOOOOMMMM!!!!

A dark sensation, but this time a subtle one that only the Tinddaya, Nikoleta and Lázaro notice, crossing their gazes in surprise, Tesla and Ampère twist their faces as they feel the rattle, Lázaro shoots and the flash shines while Nikoleta hurriedly asks them to turn a little more, the scientists turn their backs to Lázaro. Lázaro quickly changes the film.

― Láz: Nikoleta, now! -the group turns, Nikoleta jumps back-. “FLASHHH” -shines again and the other five scientists disappear. Lázaro drops the film and the camera in his pocket, pronouncing her name. He turns to her friend, who is staring unblinkingly out of the glass and into the street-. Collons!!! (Fuck!!) -he mutters overwhelmed.

Directly in front of the hotel there is a large roundabout with a large fountain from which the shadows, like a geyser, are flowing in all directions. No one seems to see them.

― Nik: Flipping in Colours! (Spanglish expression meaning shock) -she says with her mouth open-. What do we do, Lázaro… What do we do? -she desperately asks grabbing his friend by the collar of his suit.

― Láz: We’re leaving now -Lázaro throws the capsules on the ground, the scooters appear, they get on them and stop in front of the doors.

― Nik: Wait, we can break the clock now.

― Láz: No, we can’t, there is no fire from Mordor.

― Nik: Grrr… FUCK! -the doors open, they both run down the ramp speeding up.

― Láz: We pull towards the river until they catch us -she nods and just as he sentences that last word several shadows move towards them.

PIIIIIIIIIIII PIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIIII PIIIIIIIIIII!!!!! A horn sounds, a black van driven by Roland and Nélida appears at full speed in the Avenida de las Cortes Valencianas, passing right in front of them, attracting the shadows’ attention.

― Nik: It’s them, they’ve come for us!!!!!

The van accelerates in a straight line in excess of 100 km.

― Láz: No! They’re going to crash, NOOOOOOO!!!!

The van crashes into the Palacio de Congresos’ entrance, causing an explosion and a cloud of glass. After a moment of silence, music is heard from inside the car, a music box is activated, slowing down the shadows.

― Nik: NOOOOOOO!!!! -she screams her soul out. Lázaro turns her face forcibly, forcing her to look at him.

― Láz: That’s it! We have to go -he says hurriedly, his voice trembling-. They did it to give us time -he says breathlessly, his whole body trembling-. NIKOLETA! -Lázaro shakes her friend to make her react-. Take the scooter and accelerate, C’MON!!!

Both of them start the scooter and speed off in the fountain’s opposite direction, there is very little traffic and they are going at the scooters’ maximum speed. They ride side by side with tears in their eyes and a discomposed look on their faces. They pass by the Iberian Lady with the shadows crashing against her, tesserae flying through the air, they continue until they leave Campanar Viejo to the side, but the shadows are already flying behind them, Mérida’s horde of henchmen keep rushing in like a great wave.

― Láz: LA PUTA MARE!!!! (For fuck’s sake) They are upon us.

― Nik: WE ARE GOING TO DIE!!! -She shouts in anguish and helplessness.

Nikoleta and Lázaro are about to reach the Ademúz’s bridge when two figures stop on the lane that the couple is following. They are two men, one wears a waistcoat and his hair loose, the other is shirtless and wears a beret tilted to one side, they are wearing kilts, they are holding something in their hands, the closer they get the better you can discern them, the taller one is holding a bagpipe, the other is twirling drumsticks with a drum hanging from his waist. Their faces are painted.

― Láz: IT’S THEM!!!! SPARK AND DEER!!!! -he says with a huge smile, catching his breath.

― Nik: WE ARE GOING TO LIVE!!!! -she exclaims in distress.

The shadows are barely fifty metres away to catch up with them. The two scooters speed past the detectives, the wind whips up their kilts, Deer bangs his drumsticks with all his might on the drum, the bagpipes give their highest pitch and a military march starts up. Deer looks at his friend, with a solemn look, as if he knows it may be the last time they see each other, Spark winks at him and blows even harder. The black ring they wear among the many others begins to give off a black halo that creeps up their arm, the darkness beneath their white skin is visible, as if poisoning their veins. A wall of light rises before them containing the enemy, like a wall of precious crystals shining down containing the darkness, “CRAAAANK, CLOOOKKK, PUGGGG!!!!” The shadows collide cracking the wall, the cracks get bigger and bigger, the men play with an unnatural force, both roar in pain with their skin almost completely covered by the shadow, the ring strangles them while the music echoes to the last corner of Valencia. “CRRAAAAAAKKKKKKSSSSS!!!!!!!!” The wall breaks into a thousand fading pieces, the beasts fall like an avalanche on the musicians silencing their melody. The shadows spill over the Turia’s ground.

The wave of acolytes is advancing along Fernando el Catolico’s Avenue, the noise of skidding wheels comes from the street that overlooksTorres de Quart, a white car joins the street in the opposite direction, accelerating at full speed. He squeezes up in parallel with the youngsters, dodging the other cars. The kids ride in their lane on the scooters, the huge median full of trees and parks prevents the vehicle from crossing and getting closer to the youngsters.

― Nél: Come on, Salma! -says Nélida, holding on to the seat and the roof handle. Salma drives with an unhinged face.

― Rol: We’ve got them now!!! Keep going all the way to the diagonal of San Vicente Martir!

Salma nods. Nélida doesn’t look up from the boys. Roland watches the wave of darkness advancing behind them. Salma overtakes the boys in height, takes the diagonal that joins the two directions, skidding, stopping the vehicle to pick up Lázaro and Nikoleta when a black comet crashes violently against the statue of the Cid Campeador that crowns the great road, the figure scatters on the road, the asphalt rises up, sunk by the bronze, abruptly cutting off the road. The boys, unable to brake, try to turn but the inertia sends them flying into the sculpture. The darkness rises up in an instant, trapping them and making them disappear to take them to another place. Salma sinks her foot on the brake, BOOOOMMM!!!!! The car crashes sideways into the sculpture. PAAAA!!! The airbags go off.

― Sal: Shit… shit… -she says with her face buried in the airbag. Nélida can barely move.

― Rol: That’s it, this is it, this is it -he says, unbuckling his seatbelt and moving forward to the seat in front of him-. Nélida, are you all right?

Ω

Checkmate Between the Ravens and the Shadows

Jimena and Alejo are on their knees, with small dark crystals stuck all over their skin, robbing them of their energy, they can barely breathe, they look like they haven’t eaten or drunk for days.

They are in a huge rectangular room, half of the walls are basically huge windows from which you can see the Valencia’s night in the distance. The other half are cyclopean walls. The architectural lines are straight, but they shift intricately, generating an uncomfortable asymmetry.

The siblings are in the centre of the room; behind them, facing the landscape, there is a chair protruding from the floor on which sits an aged and hunched-over Mérida.

― Mérida: The watch is on its way, it has taken a little longer than expected, but it returns to its rightful owner -she pauses and with great effort stands up, takes two steps towards her captives, with an order given with her hand, Alejo and Jimena are pushed by the shadow and end up in front of the Empress-. I preferred not to look at myself in the mirror, however, my voice, my hands… -she says languid and expressionless, her eyes lost. Alejo and Jimena hold her gaze-. Bring her in! -the couple pay attention with surprise.

The metal door on the wall opens, a pale woman, gasping for breath, with bruises on her arms and legs and dried blood in her nose and ears, enters the room, pushed by the winged shadow.

― Jimena: Edisa!!!?!? -she turns and whispers to her brother. She’s not dead…

― Alejo: DAMN YOU, MÉRIDA! -he exclaims when he recognises her, the veins in his neck and face get swollen, while he digs his nails into the ground-. It’s Alejandra, her daughter!

― Mér: Calm down, Alcabre Tacoronte brothers, I only asked her to help me see possible futures with her mother’s relay, although I don’t like the future I’m facing, perhaps the daughter is not as eloquent in her visions as Edisa. Nevertheless, this seems my best chance, if not my only one… -she slurs, with a wave of her hand the crystals vibrate on Jimena’s skin and lift her face so that she looks directly at the Empress-. Thanks to me, you live in peace and protected from the steppes. You have never been a rival for me, you have never dared to stand against the Empress of Darkness, you have chosen to die as slaves rather than honour who you are -she says, jutting her jaw forward, opening her hands and spreading her fingers and then closing them tightly, both of them looking down in shame-. It’s in your your hands now -she approaches Alejandra.

― Ale: IF YOU TOUCH HER, I’LL KILL YOU!!!

― Jim: Alejandra, what have you done?!

An amorphous sphere of darkness forms in the centre of the room, between the Empress and the brothers, gaining volume, like a storm of clouds. POOOOHHHH!!!! A thud echoes off the walls, Nikoleta and Lázaro appear out of the darkness and fall to the ground with a loud thud. Nikoleta breaks her lip and two teeth, it starts to bleed. Lázaro has bent his hand, he groans in pain, but rushes to embrace her friend. They both look around them, shocked by the pain and by the strong bewilderment of travelling in the shadows. They say nothing, their breathing quickens. Mérida approaches them slowly, until she stops in front of both of them.

― Mér: Welcome -Lázaro and Nikoleta stand still, looking at each other, stunned, looking at Alejo, at a grey-haired lady next to him, and at a woman held by the winged shadow behind her-. Which of the two was it?

― Lázaro: Me -he hesitates fearfully, as if he could make amends for the pain of the people around him by confessing-, but I didn’t mean to, I really didn’t mean to -he says with a trembling voice.

― Mér: Come closer -she says impassively, without emotion. Lázaro approaches.

― Ale: Mérida, don’t hurt him! -she says with all her rage, trying to get up. The glass prevents her from doing so. Tears fall helplessly-. NOOOOO!!! Let him go! BITCH! -Lázaro stumbles in distress at Alejo’s words.

― Mér: Show me your hands, boy -she takes them and looks carefully-, you have a very high Teraghe`n. Now I understand why you managed such a feat. You are very skillful -she looks at his right hand, the woman extends her index finger and draws a shadow pattern on the boy’s palm.

― Ale: MÉRIDAAA!!! NOOOOOOOOOOOO!!!!! -screams as he digs his fingers into his bleeding fists.

― Jim: No, no, no, no, noooo… -she mumbles with tears in her eyes and saliva dripping from her lips.

The drawing penetrates the skin and dissolves as the veins run black, the skin cracks, the fingers twist in an unnatural way and the elbow bends “AAAAAAAAAAAAAHHHHHHHH!!!!” He cries out loud, hunched-over in pain. Lázaro’s eyes bug out of his socket while he looks at his hand. The right arm crumbles like ashes, the boy falls to the ground fainting from shock, he is not bleeding, the wound is cauterised. Alejo tries to shake off the glass covering his whole body, with all his strength he tries to get up.

― Nik: NOOOOOOO!!!!! -she shouts, approaching Lázaro and taking him in her arms-. WHAT HAVE YOU DONE TO HIM?!!!!!

― Mér: Cause and consequence. Give me the watch -Nikoleta takes the watch out of her partner’s fanny pack and gives it to her without thinking. Mérida approaches Jimena with it in her hand-. My possible futures depend on your answer. Fix it.

― Jim: I made the key to destroy it -she says through her tears, with an angry gesture-, there is no way back -Mérida stands before her, without changing her gesture, offering her the device. Jimena takes a deep breath, raises her head and with a kindly gesture speaks-. Destroy it and live for the time you have left. Be sensible -Alejo watches his sister’s jaw clench.

― Ale: She doesn’t deserve your mercy -he mutters angrily.

Mérida continues to age, faster and faster, holding her position waiting for a positive response from the traveller.

― Mér: Jimena…

― Jim: I can’t, Mérida, destroy it now! -the Empress changes her countenance to a warm, human one with a look of lucidity in her eyes. She looks at the watch, clasps it in her hands and a violet storm of shadows and rays of darkness crushes over the device. Nothing happens.

― Ale: It’s Mithril!!! You need fire from Mordor, you ASSHOLE!!!!

The Empress seems to grow in size, a halo of darkness ounces from every pore, her eyes expand and her gesture stretches out in a driven, impetuous gesture. A wind rises in all directions. Beams of shadow impact on her hand and reduce the watch to dust. The shadows dissipate in an instant. Her eyes have gone wild, she slouches and falls to her knees.

― Mér: It’s happening… Jimena -she mumbles, barely able to articulate-. The watch doesn’t exist anymore, but she ages even faster.

― Jim: The watch and you were one, your body is ageing 30 years in seconds -the Empress stares at Jimena-. You will live -she says nervously, not knowing what to do, not expecting that reaction to destroying the watch.

― Mér: Jimena, I didn’t want this future -she whispers turning into a mummy.

― Jim: It’s not possible, it’s destroyed!

― Ale: HA, HA, HA, YOU FORGOT THE BOOK, MÉRIDA! Won’t you destroy it? -the woman searches with her eyes, the book is next to the throne, trembling she raises her hand and with a last effort she concentrates her energy to make it burn, turning it into ashes, the old woman falls to the ground on her face, without the strength to sit up-. The ageing process seems to have stopped. Brava! Motherfucker -Mérida makes an effort to raise her hand against Alejo to silence him.

― Mér: Alejo Alcabre… -she says with a slurred voice, saliva hanging from her lips as she looks at the old man feebly-. Shadow…lift me up -she orders with a trickle of voice, the winged shadow leaves Alejandra and lifts his mistress.

― Jim: Alejo, stop it! -the woman exclaims against her brother who is still laughing.

― Ale: No, Jimena, she has killed with my book, she has written in blood on its pages -he exclaims voicelessly-. It’s not the book, Jimena, it’s the dead that are coming for her! -he sentences, at this point as if he was out of his mind.

― Mér: I am dying -she says almost lifelessly with a last breath of humanity, still in the arms of the winged shadow that is becoming less and less corporeal.

― Jim: Free me!!! -a subtle gesture from the Empress and the crystals of both brothers fall to the ground, they are free from the shadow, Jimena gets up as best she can, her body has no strength to stand upright after being for so long in the shadow, she takes two steps and falls to the ground.

ZZZZZZZZZZ!!! ZZZZZZZZZZZZZZZZZZZZZZZZZZZZ!!!!! A low humming

sound and the room is deafened, the image is distorted, a strong pressure it’s felt on them, they put their hands on their heads, they find it hard to breathe and keep their balance. Alejandra falls to the floor collapsed, pushed by the force, ZZZZZZZZZZZZZZZZZZZZZZZZZZZZZZZZZZ!!!! The winged shadow has almost disappeared, leaving the Empress on the ground with only a thread of life left to fade in her last exhalations. ZZZZZZZZ!!!!! A black hole opens above the window, the silhouette of three figures is displayed on the background, they step forward until the light from the hall illuminates them, they cross the threshold stepping into the present. The three puppets stop, the one in the middle turns his head searching for the Empress. His jaw moves orthopaedically as a human voice from the past is heard.

― Fayac: -the voice echoes, mournful and metallic- Empress, I come in the name of the Wayota, my lords have sent me to offer you eternal life in exchange for absolute obedience. What is your answer, Mérida Livia Boudicca?

― Ale: FUCK YOU BASTARDS!

― Jim: Mérida, don’t do it! Don’t become one of them -she pleads in tears.

― Mér: -looks at Jimena tenderly- Give me time -she shakes her head and lets a tear fall-. Fayac, I’ll take the deal -the life slips from her like a coal-black snowflake out of her mouth and her body turns into a blackened skeleton.

― Jim: NOOOOOOOO!!! -she gasps, trying to move towards her, reaching out to catch her breath.

A loud bang BROOOOOMMM!!! shakes the room, cracking the floor as the darkness seeps through the masonry. Everyone is silent, the shadows cover the wall until it disappears, FFFFZUZZUZZUUUUUUUZUUZUZUZUZFFF!!!, the room is deafened by the buzzing sound. Three figures loom against the background, flanked by humanoid shadows. Radon’s eyes widen in surprise at the sight of the blind hunters as he shouts “HIL ITZAZUE TXAKURRAK, ORAIN!!!” (kill the dogs, now) pointing at them. The shadows pounce like arrows at the blind hunters, ignoring the rest of the beings in the room. The two puppets waiting behind the one that’s speaking launch themselves at their attackers, unsheathing their falcatas (swords), blades glowing with a rubescent glow and blasting away the enemy shadows.

The three crows raise their hands, amorphous circles are being drawn in the air full of gibberish “ZATOZ NIGANA, INFERNUKO DEABRUA, GAUEKO!” (come to me underworld demon), they exclaim over and over again. Pillars of black fire concentrate on the puppets, stopping their attacks. The battle turns into a storm of darkness that rages in front of the blind hunters. Mercury throws an electric bolt of lightning at the puppet through which the fayac speaks. The mannequin’s sockets ignite with crimson fire. The wooden hand stretches out, the strings give way, tripling its wingspan to reach the last breath of the Empress. Mercury launches himself turned into a great raven towards the puppet!!! The wood of the blind hunter begins to burn, Mercury and the puppet are about to catch Mérida’s breath, just a few centimetres away. CLACK! The arm chain breaks, the hand closes over Mérida’s breath, the puppet gives a strong pull on itself and the arm shoots out towards the portal under Mercury’s avid gaze NOOOOOOOOOOOOOO!!!!, screams the raven. The portal closes with a thud GOOOUUUMMMM!!!! Just then the puppets fall to the ground inert, shattered, burning under the shadows. The crows stop.

― Arsenic: MULAGUA!!!! DAMN YOU!!!! -he shouts, cursing the fayac, throwing punches in the air.

― Mercury: ITZUL ZAITEZTE INFERNURA!!! (go back to hell) -he shouts with a frown, clenching his hands and his horde of demons vanish with a gust of wind.

The crows stare at Mérida’s corpse. The rest of Tinddaya watches them without saying anything, Radon approaches the body, places his hands over it, palms open, without touching it.

― Jimena: Radon -she says with a pleading gesture-, she doesn’t deserve more. Let me bury her like a Tinddaya.

― Radon: I will bury it necromancer -Jimena breathes calmly. The raven moves its hands over the body, black circles are drawn on the ground, the body sinks little by little until it disappears into the rock. The circles are engraved on the surface. Radon stands up and walks towards his brothers.

― Jimena: Thank you.

― Mercury: Without its essence it is worthless -he says with an injected look, trimmed under his eyebrows in anger-. The antediluvians will be a greater danger.

― Ale: You’re Tinddaya, stop fucking around and fight with us!

― Arsenic: We are necromancers, we do not live in subjugation to the steppe like you.

Mercury bows his head and turns around, his brothers do the same. They cross the wall of darkness and in a few seconds the black mist returns to rock. Lázaro, Nikoleta, Alejandra, Jimena and Alejo remain in the room.

Alejandra crawls towards Jimena, who is staring blankly at Mérida’s bed. Alejo looks for Nikoleta and Lázaro.

― Alejandra: Jimena, are you hurt? -she looks at her, checking her condition.

Nikoleta is holding Lázaro in her arms, shaking him and sobbing “wake up, wake up!”.

― Alejo: Calm down -wrapping an arm around the girl-. He’s alive -he can feel the boy’s heart as he puts his hand on his chest-. And you, can you move?

― Nik: Yes -she sobs covering her bloodied face.

― Jim: -regains consciousness for a moment, looks around, contemplating the scene-. Look for the exit.

― Alejandra: Yes, I’m coming -the girl stands up, drops her head back with her mouth and eyes half-open, after a while she comes back to herself-. I’ve got it.

Ω

Several days later everything seems to have changed. The afternoon is sunny, the atmosphere is calm, among the Tinddaya no one wears the ring anymore. The skills of the lineages are put to use again. Everyone knows that the Empress has fallen and that the Alcabre Tacoronte are alive.

Ω

There are several cars at the entrance, parked under a huge jasmine tree, they are in a mountain’s house. Surrounded by immense pine forests and large rocky peaks, in the distance you can see the course of the river Serpis. Breaking the line created by the mountains there is Cocentaina’s Castle.

In the house, the windows are open and the summer light floods in. The murmur of the forest mingles with music, the record player plays:

“Ooh yeah, yeah

At last, the skies above are blue

My heart was wrapped up in clover

The night I looked at you

I found a dream, that I could speak to

A dream that I can call my own”

…

There are several people in the room. On the table there is a tray with flour coke, a pot of coffee and a pot of tea. There are more than twenty people, conversations are exchanged, there is a serious tone in the atmosphere. Roland and Nélida talk to Alejandra, the girl still has some bruises, they talk about her parents, Joan and Edisa. Roland has a split lip and a lump on his forehead, Nélida is wearing a wrist brace and two fingers are held in a splint. Salma has one arm in a sling, she is sitting on the window sill.

Jimena is in a wheelchair, still unable to walk, mumbling to herself, but not talking to anyone, she is in a corner, lost in thought and trembling. Alejo watches his sister from time to time, he holds an automatic pen in his hand, presses the button a few times, turns it around his fingers, again.

There is an older woman, with curly blonde hair, green eyes, she must be in her late 80s, and she is the only one who comes and goes to the kitchen. On every trip she appears with food in her hands. She speaks exclusively in Valencian, even when spoken to in English or Tinddaya.

― Woman: Mare què bé!!! Tots ací de nou. Jo ja que em que em pensava que em moriria sense veure al meu fill. (Oh my god, how nice! All here again. I thought I was going to die without seeing my son again).

― Roland: Máma, m ́alegre de que estigues contenta, però es la tercera vegada que passes un per un dels convidats pa ́dir-los la mateixa frase. (Mum, I’m glad you’re happy, but it’s the third time you’ve passed by the guests one by one to say the same thing to them).

― Woman: Eres un fill de ton pare, que en pau descanse. No pots callar-te i deixar-me dir el que vullga? (You are a son of your father, may he rest in peace. Can’t you shut up and let me say whatever I want).

― Nél: Mariu, no li faça cas al seu fill, ja sabem com és -he winks at Roland-. Vosté xarre el que vullga. (Mariu, don’t listen to your son, we know what he’s like, you can say whatever you want).

― Mariu: No li cuques un ull, que t’ha vist (don’t wink at him, I’ve seen you).-says sitting down on the armchair, she takes out some knitting needles. She looks at her son sulkily and continues talking to herself. Salma approaches her and makes conversation.

― Nél: Go on with what you were saying about Joan.

― Rol: I’m sorry -he pauses and continues to tell Alejandra about the moments he remembers with Joan and Edisa.

A man appears down the corridor and stops at the doorway of the hall. The others listen in silence.

― Rol: Lago, how is the child?

― Lago: Very well, stable and a bit more animated. The mechanical arm has adapted 87%, with his mental abilities, if he continues like this, he will have it under control soon.

A few moments later, Lázaro appears with Nikoleta.

The boy appears limping, looking tired, he gives a half-smile. Salma gets up and approaches them.

― Sal: Good to see you awake.

― Nél: Nikoleta, how are you?

― Nik: I feel better.

― Nél: And the mouth?

― Nik: Good -the lip still has a couple of stitches, but the teeth are repaired.

― Ale: Lázaro, I’m sorry it happened like this -he says, staring at the mechanical arm.

― Láz: No passa res, Alejo (it’s okay). It’s a robotic arm with 30 times more strength, it’s cool, I’m like Edward Elrick and Luke Skywalker -smiles.

An engine sounds approaching the house, the car stops and the car door closes. A woman looks out of the front door, knocks “Knock, knock”.

― Ale: Cornelia! -he exclaims when he sees her, the woman smiles at him as Jimena looks at her with attention.

Cornelia carries a box in her hand, approaches Jimena to hand it to her, takes it and puts it on her lap.

― Ale: It’s good to see you -he comes over and hugs her.

― Cornelia: Me too, Alejo -she hugs him back with half a body hug, dodging.

― Nél: You’ve changed so much -she says, hugging her.

― Cor: Age -she looks at everyone present and waves. I see familiar faces and new ones.

― Sal: -introducing everyone and leaving the young people for last- They are Nikoleta and Lázaro.

― Cor: Hey -they both nod-. You have to tell me how you did it -she says dryly.

― Nik: Sure.

Cornelia returns to stand next to Jimena.

― Sal: -she approaches the two young men and whispers- For 29 years she has been hiding in hidden cliffs. With nothing at all, herding goats for other shepherds in exchange for food. She’s special -she nods to herself and looks back at them-. Tell them why you went all the way to Galicia.

― Cor: I hid to protect the last thing we had left after Mérida destroyed everything. Everything I was able to save is stored in that little box -she says with a cold expression-. I only hope it will do some good now that we have the chance -she turns and looks carefully at the box Jimena is holding in her lap-. It’s been many years guarding the plans of those inventions and the most relevant texts of the Tinddaya, there are hundreds of times when I thought of throwing them into the sea.

― Jim: Thank you -she says in a whisper-. I want to use the clock of ages on you -she pauses, opens the chest and searches through the various objects and papers, all looking around curiously-. Here it is -she looks towards the doctor-. Lago, Spark and Deer?

― Lago: Sleeping, but no longer under sedation. I can wake them up and bring them here.

― Jim: Yes -Lago heads into the room, Roland goes after him.

A few minutes pass and the detectives appear in wheelchairs, pushed by the doctor and Roland. They have a deteriorated appearance, they have no hair, and their skin is burnt, full of scars.

― Jim: This is the Ages’ Watch -she says, showing the watch in her hands-, it’s used to fool time and the body, we’ll look young -she pauses and a shaky sigh escapes through her lips. She lifts her head and looks at them steadily-. But we won’t be eternal and it won’t give us more years to live, we’ll still be our real age.

Spark, Deer, you’re going into the time vortex -the boys nod-. Stand around me. Lago, Nikoleta, take Spark and Deer out of the circle when their scars are gone -they both nod. Jimena hangs the watch around her neck and turns the screw to activate it.

A gust of wind comes through the window, the spires spin rapidly, hunched backs sit up, skins tighten, scars disappear. Spark and Deer return to their appearance, Lago and Nikoleta pull the two chairs separating the boys from the circle, breathing heavily. Cornelia watches them, hesitates for a few seconds and leaves the circle, looking a few years younger.

The rest is still in the circle, changing its appearance, a few seconds later the hands stop turning. A moment of stillness, the watch has stopped ticking. The others look at them with an astonished look on their faces.

― Sal: Roland, Nélida! -she exclaims, hugging her sister.

― Deer: -contemplates her arms- Tapadh leat (thank you), Jimena.

― Spark: Tapadh leat.

― Jim: -turns and looks at the detectives- Right.

Spark and Deer return to the room. Nikoleta and Lázaro watch in surprise. Salma takes off her wig and throws it into the fireplace, then reaches over and takes hers from her sister and Roland.

― Sal: Oh, to hell with it! -she says, emptying her glass of cider.

― Mariu: Oh, Roland! El meu fill (my son) -she exclaims, getting up from the armchair and hugging her son with great emotion.

― Nik: I didn’t imagine you like this -she says, approaching Salma, Nélida and Roland, but she stops especially at Alejo.

There is a bittersweet feeling. One of the men in the room stands up and introduces himself to the others.

― Koiki: Now is the time to let the Tinddaya know that we must stand together and be stronger than ever. Let those who ignore their nature, wake up -he says in a solemn tone-. The Empress’ era is over. Now it is the time of the Tinddaya. We will re-found “Eritea the New” and give rise to the development of our civilization -they nod silently.

Jimena swallows, gives him a faint smile that doesn’t reach his eyes, and pushes the chair out onto the porch. The rest of them follow her with their eyes until she disappears through the door. They are silent for a few seconds.

― Nél: And as for you -he says to Lázaro and Nikoleta-, this is your home, but I understand that you may have a place to return to.

― Nik: Whaaaat? -she says with an ironic and offended gesture-. I adore Felisa, but if you offer me a different life, I’ll just go back to say goodbye and find someone to look after her.

― Nél: Then you’re home.

― Láz: But wait, do we all have to stay in this house?

― Nél: We can start again wherever we want -Nikoleta and Lázaro look at each other with a complicit, satisfied look.
