― Deer: Spark!

― Spark: I’ll not have the military. I cannot be doing with those people. That’s the end of it.

― Salma: Okay. We are all here participating, so are you. We are building this together.

― Spark: I told you already, no military! -he exclaims and walks briskly out of the Mine to take the road that leads to his house. Salma stares at him.

― Deer: It’s a private thing. What he meant was the power they’d have to be making the decisions -he says, affected by the situation-. His father was navy, and a fierce temper on him. At nineteen the two of them had words, and he walked out of the house, and he’s not heard from him since.

― Salma: I didn’t know… What about her mother?

― Deer: With his mother, aye. The pair of them separated not long after he left the house.

― Salma: I understand.

― Deer: Well, I’ll away. Good luck the morn with the search.

Ω

Alejo walks down the hillside, watching his sister in the distance, accompanied by Nélida. Jimena has already seen them, she continues to pick up things she keeps in her pockets, a leaf, a twig, a stone.

― Alejo: That reminds me of when you collected stones as a child and kept them in a jar -Jimena stops for a second, drops the stone and continues walking.

― Nél: This part of the river is beautiful, I understand why you like to walk over here. I’m waiting for spring to see it all in bloom -she says, catching up with Jimena.

Alejo stays behind, bends down and picks up the stone his sister has dropped, puts it in his pocket and walks after them.

Ω

The following day.

― Lázaro: I want to go with you, yesterday I missed the fighters, you always bring molt xula (very cool) people.

― Nikoleta: Come on, but I thought you were on it full-time.

― Láz: Yeah… but I feel like going outside too.

― Nik: Salma is waiting, let’s go.

They go to a bridge door, open it and walk across. The door leads to a storeroom with cleaning supplies, they walk through and come out into a museum-like exhibition hall.

― Láz: What’s that…? -he looks with an amused grimace at a car with oranges falling to the ground.

― Nik: Modern art -she replies with a very dignified gesture.

― Láz: Uuuh… and since when do you know about modern art?

― Nik: Well, in this particular case, since I’ve been eating orange juice for breakfast.

― Láz: You’re so silly -he laughs.

― Salma: Keep your voices down, we’re in a museum. Don’t laugh at that, the best is yet to come -the two friends look at her expectantly. They continue walking towards the exit.

― Láz: Well, I like the car better -he says when he sees a blue staircase that leads nowhere and a lattice of wooden spoons.

― Sal: We are going to the Mosque that will really blow your minds -the receptionist looks at them curiously, she doesn’t rembember seeing them go in-. See you later, have a nice day -she says as they leave.

― Nik: Why did you put the door here?

― Sal: Between the Miguelito bar and the Centro de Creación Contemporánea, I opted for something quiet and diaphanous.

― Láz: What?

― Sal: High ceilings, straight walls, filtered light, silence… the opposite of a bar full of men watching football.

― Nik: Is today’s target too far away?

― Sal: -she pulls out the device and turns it on- Well, it seems so, because it’s not on the screen yet, but the Trisib have located him near the botanical gardens, I’m sure he likes flowers and his name is Jacinto -she says laughing at her own joke.

The three of them walk along the path of Guadalquivir’s river, crossing the San Rafael bridge. The communicator rings, Nikoleta picks up with the loudspeaker on.

― Nik: Hi…

― Uriel: Code red -he says, cutting the girl off-. Uriel speaking, we have a very high level Tinddaya, very high. He’s there, in Córdoba, near Jardín de los Naranjos (Orange tree´s garden). You have to go and get this prodigy.

― Sal: Uriel, how much is high level?

― Gabriel: We don’t know exactly, in fact, it vibrates in a peculiar frequency, maybe it’s from an extinct lineage, an alchemist, I don’t know…

― Sal: A necromancer?

― Azazel: Not at all, they make noise. The vibration of the Teraghe`n of this Tinddaya is unheard of.

― Nik: Is it dangerous? -looks at the homing device. It doesn’t appear on the screen.

― Uri: It lets its essence show in a very high frequency, it’s as if it wants only us to see it -he says the last word and the device clicks and smoke comes out.

― Sal: The homing device has burnt out -she says uneasily.

― Gab: He is calling us, as soon as you have him located, we will occupy the minds of the three of you.

They head towards the Mosque, which is not far away. They are at the gates of the Orange Garden when Uriel calls again.

― Uriel: He is not here, he is directing us. We are going to tell you streets until we find him. The three of us are going to be connected with you, okay? -all three mentally affirm.

― Azazel: Follow the pavement to almost the end of the Mosque, a little before there is a street called Velázquez Bosco, go along it. Halfway down the street you will find Calleja de la Flores, go down this street.

― Gabriel: It has to be there. Be careful.

The three of them move forward, the street is silent, unlike the previous ones, where there was more bustle. They arrive at a very small square, full of geraniums and jasmines that fill the little corner. There are a couple of cats watching the pigeons drinking from the small fountain. There is a souvenir shop on the corner, a woman with ash-blonde hair, dressed in a white dress and a hemp hat, holding a newspaper in her hand and a small flower pot magnet in her other hand. She looks at the three and smiles at them with her eyes, takes a few steps and sits on the edge of the fountain, opens the newspaper and reads. The three of them look at her.

― Uriel: It’s her -Azazel and Gabriel also confirm this.

― Nik: Can you feel it? -she asks in thought-. She’s got me totally wiped out.

― Gabriel: Lázaro, responsiveness?

― Láz: I don’t know… -that’s his answer and his only feeling.

― Azazel: It’s fascinating… -she says to herself.

― Uriel: She is waiting. Nikoleta, do you dare to come closer?

― Nik: I’m coming -decisive and fearful, she approaches. The woman looks up. Nikoleta stops. She touches the edge of the fountain with her hand, inviting her to sit down, and Nikoleta sits down next to her.

― Woman: -closes the newspaper, unhurriedly and languidly, looks at Lázaro and Salma, stops at Nikoleta’s eyes, observes them for a moment and in a deep and serene voice speaks- Newspaperrr next Friday are, therrre will be an explosion and morrre than 300 people will die in the firrre -she says in a strong Eastern European accent-, it will be a heating failurrre, you will have to save those livesss -she unfolds the newspaper and lays it in Nikoleta’s lap, pointing to the date on the copy. The girl looks at it, it’s from next Tuesday.

― Nik: -she’s speechless- Is she from the future…? -the woman nods. Nikoleta looks at her companions in surprise.

― Gabriel: She is a traveller. Ask her who she is.

― Nik: I’m Tinddaya too, so are they -she says pointing to her companions. You called us… Who are you?

― Woman: My name arrre Hajnalka Borbála (two first names: dawn and strange), Me belong to Szapolyai-Család’s house. Whetherrr Tinddaya awakens, old shadows and vampirrres frrrom the past awake as well. It would be betterrr not to awaken anyone and live in hiding, new Tinddaya will fall as they have all fallen beforrre.

― Nik: No… it’s not like that… -she says awkwardly, puzzled by the woman’s bluntness.

― Hajnalka: Evil forrrces will neverrr disappearrr and you will neverrr be ready enough to face them. To destrrroy vampirrres, it will take rebirrrth, it will take chaos.

― Nik: -looks at her fascinated- Come and meet the rest.

― Hajnalka: Show me the way to yourrr house, me want to meet mestizos -she says, placing her hand on the girl’s forehead, Nikoleta shudders back, a sparkle on her forehead-. Now me know wherrre you live. Find you when it’s safe. Betterrr to leave now.

Hajnalka stands up, nods her head in farewell, walks towards the alley opposite to the one the others have come down. A few steps to disappear around the corner. Lázaro runs and stops in front of the alley, the woman is gone.

― Láz: Molt rara… (very weird) -he says, rubbing his head, feeling normal again.

― Sal: I can still feel it -she says, taking a couple of steps towards Nikoleta-. Are you okay?

― Nik: Yes… -she stands up and clenches her fist tightly around the newspaper-. Let’s get out of here, please.

― Uriel: She is no longer there, her presence has completely disappeared.

― Sal: You have permission to go straight in if it’s that woman again.

― Gabriel: Understood.

The three of them walk in silence. They go to another street and look for a small corner where there is a doorway, Salma takes out the nexokey. She opens the door and they appear in As Talladas.

― Salma: I need to see the Trisib, will you come with me? -the couple nods.

They enter a large, open-plan room, with nothing on the walls, just the windows overlooking the valley on one side of the room. There is an armchair on which hangs a knitted scarf with its two needles and a ball of wool. A huge pouffe with a portable console with the Space Invaders game on pause and a small futon with a walkman and headphones next to it. The Trisib are standing in front of the door.

― Sal: -enters and pauses, looking at the Trisib- I’m not a mentalist, but I feel sick after having been in front of that woman.

― Uriel: We cannot give you an answer, we have felt what you felt, and we have felt it with our own perception.

― Gabriel: Come closer, Nikoleta -he says, stretching out his arms. Gabriel is almost blind, his whitish eyes nevertheless look directly at the girl. Nikoleta comes closer, the young man touches her face, her hands-. She didn’t even leave you her halo, he was just calling us. How marvellous…

― Azazel: Lázaro -the young man already knows what he has to do, he approaches and puts his hand on the girl’s chest, she shakes her head with slight spasms-. She has left a mark on you… -he touches his bionic arm-, it talks about life… how curious…

― Uriel: She is the most curious Tinddaya of the awakened. We have to communicate it.

“Knock knock knock” Roland peeks out from behind the half-open door. Salma welcomes him by showing him the search engine.

― Rol: You’ve found someone special, but from the looks on your faces I’m not sure what to expect -he says, picking up the device, looking at it quizzically. What happened to it?

― Sal: It’s blown up.

― Rol: They’re in the room, let’s go and tell us what happened, please.

Once in the room, the Trisib and the other three narrate what happened up to the moment when Gabriel and Azazel see the impregnations of Nikoleta and Lázaro.

― Lago: Nikoleta, can I see the newspaper? -the girl brings it to him. The doctor takes a look at it with Koiki next to him reading it too.

― Koiki: Uriel, can you feel the future in the newspaper?

― Uri: The paper is real, that paper has gone through a time portal -Koiki leans back on the couch and looks deeply into the situation.

― Alejo: If it’s a traveller, having an object from the future is a very high Teraghe`n. But what really makes me concerned is the thought of her being able to cross a portal a week into the future, that can annihilate you, it’s a lot more fucked up than travelling into the past.

― Koiki: -looks at Jimena, who is completely oblivious to the conversation- Jimena, a necromancer, perhaps?

― Jim: If antediluvians can’t cross living things, no necromancer can -she goes back to her thoughts, the others still talking-. Look for her family -she interrupts.

― Lago: On the net? -nods. Lago looks at his mobile phone-. This woman has surnames from the 15th and 16th century. The family and its lineage exist.

― Ale: A leap in time from the 16th century to today? Impossible -they all wait for the conversation between the travellers. Alejo walks looking at his sister, surrounding the others from behind the seats-. What if she is able to preserve herself in time?

― Koiki: What kind of traveller are we dealing with?

― Jim: Extinct.

― Nélida: So it has come from the past to us?

― Lago: Body preservation seems a viable answer. But it does not explain the paper she had.

― Roland: If Mérida had known of Hajnalka’s existence, she would have killed anyone to dominate that woman.

― Alejo: -he frowns with discomfort- If it has lasted for 5 centuries, its Teraghe`n is powerful enough to travel to the future and endure it. If it came straight from the past and could roam freely in our present, we’d be talking about Naga abilities.

― Nél: What are our options regarding her? I don’t want another diva in my life.

― Ale: Henceforth, Tinddaya who appears with these powers and does not show themselves, will not be allowed to enter As Talladas or the Mine -he says with an emphatic gesture.

― Koi: It’s decided then.

― Ale: Well, if we all agree, it’s over. Salma, let’s go to the library.

― Sal: Done, I know where to look.

Ω

Six days later.

The fighters spend hours training alongside others of their lineage. At night they continue their training with Koiki in the garden. During the breaks they spend time in the library among the books. A few hours before dawn they return home.

The rest of Tinddaya, especially those who learn outside the Mine, can see the sisters from sunrise to sunset, it is rare that anyone is left to meet them, the ability of the fighters to develop their technique is an exercise in tireless perseverance.

― Roland: Nakano, Joanna, it’s fascinating to see the interest you are showing, but you spend too much time training, you can notice that nobody spends as many hours here as you do. Training is as important as your rest.

― Joanna: There’s no turning back now that we know the whole Tinddaya story. Our lineage will be great again -she bites her teeth fiercely.

― Nakano: We have resigned from the army. From now on we will be here training day and night.

― Rol: Me and all of us find your attitude fascinating, and I applaud your decision. It is a source of pride. True lineage talent comes from hard work. I’ll let you continue -he takes two steps and stops-. We didn’t offer, I know you live together, but you could live here, each with your own space.

― Joanna: That’s fine, As Talladas is nice, but we like living in Green Bay, in our grandparents’ house.

― Rol: As you wish.

Ω

After the intense talk about the traveller, the next day there is a consensus to repair the boilers that supposedly will explode during the three days of the national base jumping convention at Torre La Rosaleda, an accident that would kill more than 100 people. It is a simple matter of replacing deteriorated gas pipes that were about to come loose at the joints. A team of 4 engineers to check the installations, Spark and Deer to check if there is any impregnation that the Trisib can examine.

Ω

Back at the Mine, Spark and Deer report to Koiki.

― Spark: There’s nothing.

― Koiki: That’s a good sign.

― Spa: I want a look at the newspaper.

― Koi: Alejo has it, look for it in the office -they both nod.

Alejo’s door is open, there is banging and a loud groaning. Deer knocks on the door, but he doesn’t hear him. They go through, inside the office there is a second door that leads directly to the gym. They go through. Alejo is punching a boxing bag, he is drenched in sweat, he pivots around the bag, he sees them. He stops and walks towards them.

― Ale: What have you seen?

― Spa: Nothing.

― Ale: -he purses his lips and nose- Fuck….

― Spa: I want the newspaper.

― Ale: It’s on my desk -he pauses under Spark’s piercing gaze-. What are you going to do?

― Spa: I want to study it properly. We’ve gadgets that came down from our great-great-grandparents.

― Deer: We’ve no notion what’ll come of it, but… we’d like fine to try -he says with an inconclusive gesture-. He’ll not say it to you, but he’s every bit as worried as yourself. Truth be told, I think the lot of us are worried -he says, looking at his friend.

― Ale: Do what you have to do. I want to be sure he is who he says he is -Spark nods. He turns and enters the office.

― Dee: Tapadh leat.

Alejo punches the bag again. Moans fill the gymnasium with their echoes.

The door-bridge opens from As Talladas, both enter to what appears be the cellar, light enters through the lateral oculi. The room is full of junk devices. Things arranged neatly on the shelves, labelled, and sorted by utility. Spark begins to rummage through the shelves, picks up various items and places them on the table, Deer goes upstairs, enters the kitchen and heats water while preparing two cups of tea. He opens the window and looks out to sea, following the escaping steam with his eyes,they live in an old house in Heilam, it is a region facing the Atlantic sea, there are hardly any dwellings among the huge meadows. There are only brown cows and a lot of green between the long masonry walls. The sky is always grey and sparkling, but the views are priceless. It is a large house, grey brick and black tiles, it is quite neglected, the vine covers it on all sides intermingling with the branches of the two trees that guard it, there is a broken swing and a leaning fountain that lets the water fall on the ground. The access road is muddy, only the A838 road reaches it. From the outside you can see that the interior is divided in two, on the one hand the windows on the right side are clean and covered with curtains embroidered with flowers, and on the other hand the windows on the left side are dirty, with broken curtains and the window sills full of leaves.

The kettle whistles. Deer comes down with the cups.

― Deer: Spark, do you think we’ve altered the fabric of the universe? That these deaths are bound to happen at some other time?

― Spark: It’s not the dead that worry me. What worries me is there’s not a trace of yon woman in the paper.

― Dee: And you leave no traces either, when that’s your intention.

― Spa: I am what I am. And by what the others said, the woman had the paper in her hand before ever she gave it to Nikoleta.

― Dee: If it’s true she’s been hiding these centuries, I doubt yon sort of detail would get past her. You saw it yourself: it was only the Trisib could pick her up.

― Spa: I’ll use the Eye of the Banshee. Tie me to the Chair.

― Dee: …Are you sure? -the other one doesn’t answer- …Aye.

Spark sits on the anchor, used to keep his body in our reality, it is an iron chair, old and heavy. He puts a necklace on his that he takes out of a rock chest, it is made of gnawed metal with a huge purplish stone. He puts the newspaper in his hands. Spark closes her eyes. Deer stands beside him.

― Spa: Mmm… mmmmmmmmmmmmmmm…… -moans as his eyes are moving at full speed under his eyelids-. MMAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAHHHHH… MMAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAHHHHH…. -he moans louder and louder until it becomes a scream that sounds like a terrible wail-. MMAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAAHHHHH… AAAAHHHHHHH -his whole body trembles, he stops suddenly, pupils dilated, a desperate aspiration, his whole body falls senselessly oozing smoke.

Deer quickly removes the necklace, puts it in the chest and closes the lock. Deer dips his hands in a basin of thick liquid, cleans himself thoroughly. He dries himself. He approaches his friend, partially unties him so that he doesn’t fall under his own weight. He dips some gauze in the same liquid and places it on the back of Spark’s neck, who slowly wakes up. He unties him completely. Spark remains in the chair with a blank stare.

― Dee: What was it you saw?

― Spa: … -Deer gestures expectantly-. Nothing… It was nothing I saw.

― Dee: And what would nothing be? You’re not making sense to me.

― Spa: The emptiness, lad.

Ω

The next morning Spark looks for Alejo, who is sitting on a cress a few metres from the Mine. It is early and there is still no one in As Talladas.

― Spark: It’s nobody. It’s empty, and that’s all.

― Alejo: -looking at him slowly- Explain.

― Spa: It means she does not exist.

― Ale: Fuck! I knew it!

― Spa: Chan eil sin math (that’s not good).

― Ale: I know -he says, throwing a stone into the void. He stands up and looks at Spark sideways-. Stay alert.

Spark turns and walks down the mountain. Alejo remains there, hovering.

Alejo comes down from the mountain a while later. He enters the Trisib common room without knocking. Uriel stands up when he sees him, dropping the headphones through which the music is playing. Azazel is lying down playing Space Invaders, pauses the game and turns around. Gabriel is the only one still knitting as he watches Alejo.

― Ale: Come on.

― Uri: What is it?

― Ale: Spark has seen the nothingness in that woman.

― Gabriel: What did he say? What did Spark see?

― Ale: I don’t know, and I don’t care. The traveller saw everything through Nikoleta, she knows where we are. Your objective from now on is to cover As Talladas and any Tinddaya hotspots. She’ll show up again -the three brothers look at each other and nod.

― Uri: All right.

― Ale: No one must know, if they ask you, you are looking for more Tinddaya to wake up.

― Aza: We won’t say anything about Hajnalka.

― Ale: -he looks at her suspiciously- The priority is to protect the Tinddaya. It’s not a game.

Ω

Salma walks with her sister down the corridor, one of the doors to the engineers’ development area is ajar, there is a rustling inside. They look in, Aecio, Eir, Lago and Ailén are visibly excited in front of a screen. There are several students reclining in their armchairs, all of them wearing helmets that cover most of their faces and suits that are sensitive to motor impulses. One of them is Lázaro. On the screen you can see how they operate their avatars, combat drones in a three-dimensional simulator. Lázaro repeatedly destroys the ships piloted by the others, who unsuccessfully try to get close to him.

― Salma: Making bets?

― Ailén: No way! We only have one winner. He’s completely mastered it, we’ve simulated a ship of 80,000 units and he’s controlling it. The simulator doesn’t follow the physics of reality exactly, this guy is exceeding the parameters.

― Eyr: The substance that links the bionic arm to his body has mutated. They are now a whole, not that it’s anything out of the ordinary, but in him it’s gone beyond.

― Lago: He has a lot of desire -he lowers his voice and covers his mouth with his hand-. Losing his arm is the best thing that has ever happened to exploit his ability.

― Nélida: He’s an incredible boy.

― Salma: Can I play too? -she asks, looking at an empty armchair. I’ll bet 1,000 pesetas that I manage to hit him.

― Ailén: Put on a suit and sit down, I’ll double the bet in your favour.

Salma, already dressed, helmeted and in the armchair, enters the simulator, her name appears on the screen next to her avatar. She pilots a single-unit ship, yet she pilots it with great speed and skill. She is unable to catch up with Lázaro but maintains very little damage.

Ω

It is nighttime, all is quiet in the village, the solar lanterns dimly illuminate the different entrances to the houses, almost in all of them the Tinddaya are asleep, except for the tree house, which has a couple of candles lit.

Cornelia sits reading under the window, her dogs asleep at her feet, chuckling, reading “There’s always time to die”. On a small piece of furniture there is one of Nélida’s music boxes, a wicker basket with a woollen flock, a couple of pebbles and a feather. The things she keeps from her former life. In the last corner there is an old photo of her parents with her as a child.

The gentle breeze sways the leaves to its sound, suddenly a brief gust of air blows out the candles and wakes the dogs up, the pages of the book that she was are now lost. Cornelia picks up the box of matches, lights the candles again, watches her dogs, who raise their heads and listen attentively, looks out at every corner of the village, nothing seems to have changed.

She watches them for a couple of minutes, they stare into the darkness, but gradually fall asleep again. Her face loses its worried expression and continues reading.

Moonlight streams in through the window. Nikoleta is lying in bed, she is asleep, she turns over, a shiver runs through her body, she has to pee, she gets up and goes into the dark bathroom. She comes out, stops and looks carefully, a shadow on the window, she looks at it restlessly, she gets closer and slowly pushes the blind with her hand, it is only a branch that casts a shadow, she looks towards the garden, there seems to be nothing, except for the small surveillance drones that float a few metres above the houses. She turns to go back to bed, and suddenly “Clack”, a noise.

― Nik: … Lázaro?… -one second no answer, she throws her hand towards the switch turning on the light-. AAAAAAAAHHH!!!!! -she screams falling on her backside, covering her mouth with her eyes out of her head, in front of her Hajnalka’s reflection in the mirror-. Fuck you, what a fright!-she exclaims standing up, looks at the mirror and looks at her side-. Hajnalka only exists on the reflected side.

― Lázaro: Nikoleta!!!! -the boy opens the door and runs towards her-. Aaahh! -he shouts when he sees the woman in the reflection-. HOSTI TU!!! (Oh my fucking god!) What are you doing there?! -they both stare at her with a discomposed look on their faces.

― Hajnalka: Not shout. I come to look forrr you, to meet yourrr people, to meet the mestizos. Me come in?

― Nik: Now, in the early morning?

― Láz: But… she’s in the mirror -he looks at the visitor in bewilderment.

― Haj: Me not know if anotherrr way be safe forrr me.

― Nik: Not you know…? Everyone’s asleep now -she says hesitantly-, everything is switched off, we can’t show her anything -they both look at each other.

― Haj: Thrrrough the mirrrorrr my ancestorrrs trrravelled, no one can penetrrrate this level, it can not be crrreated orrr destrrroyed, the worrrld upside down is my worrrld.

― Nik: Can she come in? Nothing will happen, right? -she looks at Lázaro, the boy replies with a shrug.

― Lázaro: I suppose… yes… -looks at Nikoleta in displeasure.

Cornelia, Roland, Salma and Alejo appear and burst the door open. The four of them enter the room breathless, frightened by the screams. They stop in surprise to enter the room and see the woman in the background, with the two youngsters in front of her. Hajnalka is crossing over to the real plane, she is in front of the group.

― Alejo: What are you doing here? -he approaches her with his eyes fixed on her.

― Salma: Are you all right? -they nod.

― Hajnalka: Me want to get to know you and wherrre you to live -the woman reaches out to touch the children.

― Alejo: -he steps forward violently and grabs her arm- Don’t touch them -Cornelia reaches over and pulls him gently. He lets go of Hajnalka.

― Rol: Let’s go to the gym -Roland leaves the room.

― Sal: I’ll stay with them -putting her arms around the children.

They leave the boys’ granary, Cornelia goes ahead, Alejo walks next to the traveller, and Roland closes the group. They walk a few metres until they reach a small stone building, he opens the door, inside it is a much larger space. He turns on the lights.

Nélida walks towards the centre of the room from another one of the doors, carrying a music box in her hands, it is different from the others, its geometry is altered.

― Uriel: Wait until you have her with you -he communicates with her mentally.

― Nél: I’m ready -she replies quietly.

Hajnalka walks towards the centre next to Alejo, he does not look up from her. Cornelia and Roland walk behind them until they reach the centre, standing on either side of them.

― Nél: Hajnalka, I’ll ask you a few questions, okay? -the woman nods. Nélida turns the crank once, stops subtly for a moment, her arm is now moved by Uriel, the melody sounds numinous and ineffable-. Is Hajnalka your name? -she nods-. Are you a traveller? -nods-. Are you hiding out of fear? -she nods. Nélida’s pupils widen-. Do you have contact with necromancers? -the woman denies-. Are your intentions towards us dark? -she denies. Nélida comes to, stops moving the lever. Alejo approaches her and whispers in her ear.

― Alejo: Uriel, are you sure?

― Uriel: -sounds in Alejo’s head- The music hasn’t altered, it has sounded harmonious, it doesn’t lie.

― Ale: Now… -Roland, seeing Alejo’s gesture, approaches his wife and takes her hand.

― Haj: -looks at everyone and stops at Alejo- Me just want to meet mestizos -she gives him a friendly smile.

― Ale: Not tonight.

― Nél: But…

― Ale: Not without talking about it first -Nélida holds back.

― Rol: Perhaps it would be better to meet another time, Hajnalka. I think it’s possible to meet under better conditions. Is that okay with you?

― Haj: Good is.

― Rol: Contact the Trisib as you did before and we will prepare your reception.

― Cor: We’ll walk you to the mirror -the woman nods.

They all walk back towards the children’s room. Koiki and Spark wait at the gym door, waiting for everyone to pass and walk behind them. They enter the house, it is empty. Hajnalka crosses the mirror and disappears.

Alejo lifts the mirror by hand and goes outside.

― Ale: Where are the children?! -he exclaims.

Salma goes outside.

― Sal: What happened?

― Ale: I want the children here.

They come out and walk stiffly towards the group, Salma behind them.

― Nik: We didn’t mean to do anything wrong… -she says with tears in her eyes. Lázaro shudderingly sniffles through his snot.

― Sal: You haven’t done anything wrong -she says, looking at Alejo-, calm down.

― Ale: Who let her in?! -they both look at each other without knowing the answer.

― Nik: …both…

― Ale: Who said yes?

― Láz: …Me…

Alejo puts the mirror down, picks up an ashlar and hands it to the boy.

― Ale: Break it!

The boy grabs it with his bionic arm, lifts it up and throws it against the mirror. Nikoleta picks up a stone and throws it against the glass as well, everyone looks at her, she shrugs her shoulders.

― Ale: Say “Hajnalka, you are no longer welcome” -Lázaro repeats the words, Nikoleta also repeats them quietly. First thing tomorrow morning, emergency meeting.

Everyone goes home. The young couple sleep with Salma tonight.

Ω

The stars flood the sky, you can see the milky way silhouetted against the dark horizon of mountains. There are no city lights, in the distance there’s the faint glow of a shepherd’s house shining in the distance. The silence is deafening, only interrupted by the gentle breeze and the sound of nocturnal animals roaming in the bushes. It is cold.

At the base of Nemrut’s burial mound, next to the huge rock carvings, there’s a bonfire still burning. Radon is gazing intently at the images that show him the orb, Arsenic throws several bones in a circle, observes the position in which they have been left. Mercury is at the zenith, far from his siblings, he seems to be waiting for someone to arrive.

In the distance, silhouetted against the moon, a shadow can be seen heading towards the tomb’s summit at full speed, the huge wings almost transparent flap, the beast’s body is fluttering, its figure resembles a disfigured bat. It approaches almost faintly, its intrusion into the real world is about to destroy it. The beast crosses at the level of the necromancer, so close that they seem to collide, it whispers a few words in Mercury’s ear, only to fall dying and disappear into the earth, leaving a light cloud of ashes.

The bonfire loses intensity, Radon picks up a couple of branches and places them on the fire, it seems to flicker several times and then abruptly goes out, a flare of flame rises, drawing the malformed face of what looks like an elephant. The two ravens leap back in attack position, Radon floats the orb in his hand while Arsenic draws shadow circles with his hands.

― Face in the fire: You don’t know what you are doing, necromancers. I warn you, return the lord of thunder, Kennin ji, to the eternal slumber and put down any attempt to awaken the children of Moor, the more harm they will cause only to what you have already begun. Nor antediluvians will be able to do anything against them. You will die.

― Arsenic: Tanuk ik, you are not welcome. Go away.

― Radon: Get out! -he exclaims, kicking the earth, raising a cloud of dust over the fire.

The face disappears and the bonfire is extinguished.

Mercury glides down the mountain. He lands with his siblings.

― Mercury: Who has appeared?

― Ars: It was Tanuk ik -Mercury keeps looking at him for him to speak-. He warned us about Kennin ji. He said Moor’s children are trying to trick us.

― Mer: That’s what they believe, that they can use us to be awakened, but without Moor’s presence, they are nothing, just slaves in our hands -he shows a vial with a dark essence inside-. Faust has given me his last breath, he intends to join us and have us give him a body on this plane. But we will not give him the same treatment as his brother Kennin ji, we will distil his essence and it will be our elixir.

― Rad: Mercury, the essence of a Moor’s child?

― Mer: The old people have Mérida. We have the old gods.

― Rad: What if something goes wrong? We’ve never drunk anything so powerful.

― Mer: Kennin ji is ravaging lives wherever he goes, international warfare is on the rise, our power increases with every wave of panic. He has sown discord among the Tinddaya. And if Tanuk ik has appeared, it’s because we are doing well.

― Arsenic: We have Cräpatos’ mask. Kennin ji and Faust are ours -he winks at his brother-. Kaixo, Bele laguna! Dei egiten dizut! (greetings, my raven friend, I summon you) -the buckles and glasses appear on him, he soars and flies.

Mercury and Radon summon their raven, and the two fly away from Nemrut.

Ω

A servant hurries through the corridors, whistles and makes himself heard, crosses the threshold of the door and enters the kings’ hall. Inside the three tubs, the figures move. The whistle was an urgent wake-up call.

― Fayac: Tamaraggwa Wayota (good morning, demon) -says the man in a broken, gangly, old voice-. Be merciful to allow my presence without asking for your permission, the news I carry is of great importance -he eyes of the three demons pop out-. Tanuk ik has presented himself to the grandchildren, Kennin ji is growing strong without their knowledge, and Faust is plotting his wiles to be awakened -the antediluvians listen intently, their ruddy eyes are glowing brighter under their eyelids-. Shall we bring Tanuk ik to this plane? -One of the elders opens his eyes.

― Wayota 1: No. Prevents Faust from entering and seals Kennin ji.

― Fayac: Be it -they don’t answer and their skulls sink back into the liquid.

The fayac (priest) walks through the corridors until he reaches a room where the knocks reverberate, the door is ajar, he pushes slowly, inside another fayac dressed in a different sackcloth carves some wooden blocks on a large table, from the block emerges what looks like feet and calves.

― Fayac: How is the carving of the Empress’ continent coming along?

― Sculptor: Simply delicious, I was waiting for such an event to carve into a blood oak. The carving will be complete in four months, but until the wood is at its maturity peak of as a container, it will take a year. I need a solar alchemist and a lunar alchemist to seal the wood, for it alone will not be able to serve as a body, and in turn serve as a container for Mérida, it will need a very powerful seal to keep her submissive to our will.

― Fayac: It will take us more than the year you demand to find and hunt down celestial alchemists.

― Sculptor: No hurry, don’t rush, the sculpture is not ready and the essence of the empress has not yet weakened enough. We must sever her spirit to weaken her and prevent her from rebelling, the subjugation rings are ready -he says, looking at the shelf where there are several casting tools and a pair of rings-. Three for them, and two for us -he says in a hissing whisper-. I know she liked to dominate the Tinddaya like that -he says with a half-smile-. Don’t hurry, Mulagua, we’ve been waiting for hundreds of years, what awaits is but the blink of an eye….

― Mulagua: I will send for the trappers, they will search for and bring the alchemists -he says as he walks towards the door-, Orone… -the sculptor looks at him expectantly-, don’t let all your knowledge be wasted on this piece.

― Orone: -she drops the tools and approaches him- I know that look, Mulagua… What are you hiding that I should know?

― Mul: The offspring have awakened Kennin ji were you aware?

― Oro: Ha ha ha… I show you that it is a knowledge I am privy to, you do not enjoy the virtue of being the only one who spies in the shadows. Tell me what you know.

― Mul: -with a wave of his hand the door closes and the shadows begin to cover the room, plunging everything into darkness- They do not work to weaken Mérida -Orone’s eyes widen with attention-. To consume it is their will, of which I suspect they intend to drink it among the three of them to increase their Teraghe`n, but I know them in excess, they will kill each other to be the only one to drink it….

― Oro: If one of them were to drink the blood of the mestizo in its entirety, they would attain a Teraghe`n hitherto unheard of. Should the Teraghe`n be more powerful than their own will, the craving would make them ungovernable and the next to be devoured would be us.

― Mul: I strongly dislike this idea, Orone.

― Oro: It would be the closest thing to a Naga in our time, there would be no creature capable of controlling it… -he pauses-. We could take advantage of the fact that the ravens have plans to bring one of Moor’s children to our time. If it were to reach the Antediluvians, it would take them out quickly.

― Mul: We don’t have the Naga masks, only with them we could control the beast.

― Oro: Right…

― Mul: Let’s forget Kennin ji and let me know what you orchestrate regarding the empress.

― Oro: The Wayota have ordered the creation of three rings, but there are five that I am working on, two will be for us without their knowledge.

As soon as the empress is distilled, the act of reincarnation will take place, each of them will receive a ring and the body will be occupied. I doubt that this moment will come to ocurre though, first they will kill each other to get the three rings, the one who survives will drink the vial with the empress’ essence and will pretend to rule her with the three rings.

Just as the antediluvian drinks the essence, at that very moment, we will awaken the empress to destroy the Wayota. We will have more essence in our rings than will be in the three Wayota rings combined. By that time, she will be introduced into the oak carving, she will be sealed with the blood of celestial alchemists, and she will be the perfect container for a submissive mestizo. We will be the new lords of darkness.

― Mul: I don’t want to underestimate the antediluvians, it would be falling into their arrogance. But it seems to me a very tempting plan. For the time being, let us leave ourselves to contemplation, and when the time comes, we will take the necessary steps to conclude these our wills.

― Oro: Delicious, Mulagua, your divided essence will be our greatest empire -he says, looking at the rings-. This conversation has never taken place -he says, unravelling the shadows, heading for the door-. Be it.

― Mul: Be it.

Ω

Koiki is sitting in front of the fire with his eyes closed, he seems not to be surprised, he has prepared the fire expecting this moment sooner or later. The fire shudders and goes out, everything is in darkness, but in the mind of the sensitive one the landscape of the forest is drawn, a barren forest, of purplish earth, of large dry trees, it is night, but the sky is clear, the woods stand out clearly against the firmament. One of the trees moves slowly, Koiki approaches it until he reaches its feet. It is an anthropomorphic figure, very tall, with the deformed face of an elephant, resembling a wood carving in the shadows. They look at each other.

― Koiki: Greetings, Cräpatos’ guardian -he bows.

― Tanuk ik: Sensitive, one of Moor’s children has been awakened, the thunder beast walks bound among men at the hands of necromancers. There is nothing you can do but await for my visit.

― Koiki: Nothing?

― Tanuk ik: No one can, only guardians.

― Koiki: Understood.

Koiki opens his eyes, he is in front of the campfire again, the fire is rekindling normally. He sits there watching the embers rise with the smoke. If there is nothing to do, it is not relevant to call out to his companions, only to watch over their lives and wait to be summoned.

Ω

The Magic of Two Wolves with No Wish to Return

8:00 a.m. in the morning of the day after Hajnalka’s visit. They are gathered in a clearing by the granaries.

― Alejo: I will not change a fucking comma of what I have written in that document!!!

― Cornelia: I agree with what it says, but don’t be a dick.

― Spark: What in God’s name! If you do that, I’m away -he says with a gesture somewhere between defiance and disappointment. Salma looks at him with regret.

― Alejo: Well, I’m really pissed off that you’re leaving, but I’m not going to let the traveller sneak in here again.

― Roland: She has breached every security system we have without being detected. Shielding our home won’t stop her from coming back, we don’t know what her skills are.

― Alejo: That’s it! If she appears again, the fighters will face her -he says, leaving the clearing and heading up towards the Mine.

― Jimena: Good -she says conclusively. She walks over to Spark and taps him on the shoulder-. Don’t go away.

― Spark: …ahm… -Jimena’s look throws him off-. Aye… -Jimena follows the path that leads to the river.

― Nélida: I understand that he is angry, because he cares about us and I know he does it to keep us safe. But from what I saw yesterday from that woman, she didn’t convey any fear. She was kind.

― Uriel: It is fascinating, it is an abyss of uncertainty and emptiness. Closing the doors to it would be a mistake, we have to have access to it.

― Koiki: I see that our balance is fragile, and this traveller is upsetting us. We need to take these decisions calmly. But in the face of potential danger, protecting ourselves is the prudent thing to do.

― Roland: -putting his hands together and taking a deep breath, looks at them all one by one- I understand that we agree that the situation is new, we don’t know how to interact with this woman and her capabilities are beyond us. Let’s set up a squad of fighters to be located in the Tinddaya centralisation zones. And also, the sisters will be alerted to intervene immediately in case Hajnalka reappears -no one disagrees.

Ω

Jimena is sitting on the side of the stream with her feet in the water, staring into the void. Cornelia arrives at her side.

― Cor: Can I sit down? -Jimena nods.

― Jim: He wants to keep us safe but he is consumed by anger.

― Cor: I know… his temper became very pronounced when you started building with Mérida and he stayed on the sidelines. I think now he’s trying to anticipate any possible threat, he wouldn’t forgive himself for losing you again.

― Jim: Yeah… But he never achieved anything by imposing himself, only to distance us.

― Cor: The suspicion was not because of the time you spent with Mérida, but because of Mérida’s idiosyncrasies. But in his own way he respected you, even though he believed she would pervert you.

― Jim: HA… I think I wanted her to do it.

― Cor: -she gives her an understanding half-smile-. The past doesn’t matter any more, the matter now is to know how to protect what we have now. It must be hard to be always alert and trying to look after everyone.

― Jim: It is. Everyone lives as a slave to their own ways.

― Cor: And you, are you a slave too? -she rubs her shoulder affectionately.

― Jim: Yes.

― Cor: Sometimes I see you talking to yourself, talking to her -Jimena looks at her in what seems to be an affirmative gesture-. Do you think she’s still alive?

― Jim: She is, very weak, on the shadow plane. They must be taking care of her essence, the intention will be to give her some kind of body, but they need much more time and that’s something only necromancers know how to do… When that time comes, yes, we must be ready -she looks away from Cornelia-, will not be the Mérida we know.

― Cor: I feel that fear in the air. I don’t think about it much because I know it’s like this and that’s all there is to it, so I try to enjoy this illusion of peace, but the balance is so fragile and the disorder is so constant, it’s really tiring.

― Jim: … … -Cornelia gives her time, Jimena dips her hands in the water as well-. …The order… The thing is that there are some things that have no order -Cornelia pays attention-. I feel it in Teraghe`n, I feel it when I think of her, there’s no order, and that’s implausible in a creature like Mérida.

― Cor: It’s very vague what you say.

― Jim: I know, but there’s no more -she takes her hands out of the water and takes a deep breath-. Buy me a cup of tea and show me that second-hand app you use to buy bracelets? I’m in the mood for some smelly converse.

― Cor: I smell like a goat, you envious. Let’s go.

Ω

Spark walks with violent, orthopaedic steps, punching the air with a stuttering sound, up and down the ruins of Juromenha. Deer is eating cherries sitting on the wall, throwing the pits into the Guadiana.

― Deer: When you’re done with that, could we away down to the village? I’ve seen they’ve a wee paper museum of geek stuff -Spark walks over to his friend. Deer waits motionless. Spark picks up the bag of cherries and shakes it out over the river-. Fuck… It’s my lunch.

― Spark: Fick, its mi linch.

― Dee: You’re insufferable -he approaches one of the doors still standing among the ruins, they cross it appearing in the ladies’ toilets of the Olivenza bus station, an old lady who is washing her hands looks at them as she comes out of the toilet-. Morning to you, my lass -he says with a wave, the lady smiles and blushes.

Outside the station. They walk following google maps’ direction to the paper craft museum. The museum is in one of the cloisters of the convent on the wall. There is an excursion of children fluttering around screaming at the sight of their favourite characters. Both of them try to get away from that group.

― Dee: What is it you’re at? -he says when he sees Spark picking up a figure.

― Spa: I like it. Dùn do chab! (shut it!)

― Dee: That’s thieving, man. Put it back where it was -while Sparks hides the figure in the backpack.

― Spa: I know… -he says, turning his back on him and walking towards the exit.

Deer looks at him indignantly. Next to the the room’s door there is a girl standing in front of a Frozen figure, looking at Spark out of the corner of her eye. She approaches him.

― Girl: I saw that, sir. Didn’t your parents teach you that stealing is wrong? -Spark looks at her with an aseptic face, without averting his gaze he takes the figure out of the backpack, the girl stares unblinkingly at him. Spark reaches forward to hand the figure to the girl, both hands touch, Spark’s gesture changes as he touches her. The girl takes the figure and puts it back. She returns to her side-. Thank you very much -and she continues to look at the Frozen figures, along with other children who begin to trickle into the room.

Spark catches Deer’s eye with a gesture and leaves the museum. They are both on the street.

― Dee: You’re a lot today, so you are.

― Spa: The lass is a necromancer -he says sternly.

― Dee: Dè?

― Spa: She doesn’t know it. Her Teraghe`n is massive -Deer shakes his head.

― Dee: Well, that’s new… We cannot lose her… Who is it we tell?

― Spa: It’s the school’s name they’ve on the shirt, so we know fine where it is. We’ll away to the Mine and speak to Salma.

Spark nods, they look for a door on which they place the knob and the access to As Talladas opens.

Deer and Spark enter the library, Salma is in front of the computer next to a pile of old books.

― Salma: Hey, what’s up guys! No matter how hard I look, I can’t find anything about this lady -she says without looking up-, I can’t believe there is no information about them except in the Yamnaya world… Although in reality those four books are the little we have that collects Tinddaya data… it’s shitty… -she throws her glasses on the desk and leans back on the armchair with a tired gesture-. Are you all right?

― Dee: We’ve found a necromancer lass.

― Sal: No fucking way -she says, smacking her forehead-. There was no better time for this.

― Spa: Not only that. She’s of a very high order.

― Sal: -with a forced smile she grits her teeth- I have the same doubt as you right now, I don’t know who to say it to.

― Dee: And what if we said nothing at all?

― Sal: No. That option is not feasible, it is withholding information and in default, giving more power to the ravens.

― Spa: If she’ll not come with us, she’ll belong to the ravens.

― Sal: Look… I don’t know, I don’t want another Mérida, I don’t want more Ravens, I don’t want classism between lineages, I don’t know what the hell I want anymore… -she stands up-. Let’s call everyone together again so we can all decide.

Ω

20 minutes later.

― Roland: The situation is very delicate, but I think this is the best time to embrace this circumstance. We are more alert than ever, let’s reinforce even more the protection of As carved, with the combatants at our side. If we awaken the child, she will learn with us. It is better than letting the ravens find her.

― Alejo: NOO, NOOO and NOOOOOOO!!!! -he shouts angrily as he gets up, pushing the armchair and kicking the table in the middle, while the rest of the people jump back and some of them get up in fright.

― Cornelia: ALEJO!!! GET OUT OF HERE, RIGHT NOW!!!! -she exclaims walking towards him with her hand raised indicating the door to the gymnasium, he looks at her motionless-. NOW!

― Ale: FUCK MY FUCKING LIFE!!!! -he says, walking towards the door and slamming it shut.

It takes a few seconds for the atmosphere in the room to calm down.

― Roland: Well… I know the decision is hard, that we don’t all like it, but I can’t think of any other option -he says with a lump in his throat.

― Spark: Tha mi duilich (I’m sorry) -he says, looking back at Jimena-. I don’t want to be here if it turns into a militarised centre. I’m still operational out of here -he walks out the door.

― Deer: He’ll not drop a single one of his tasks; I’ll come and go -he says, standing in his place in sorrow.

― Nélida: Don’t worry. You have knobs and earpieces, and the Trisib can always locate you. Thanks for your patience, Deer.

― Dee: Tha e ceart gu leòr (it’s all right).

― Koiki: The child will be with those who do not feel rejection towards her, at least for now, that feeling cannot be present. If there is to be a choice for a necromancer to grow up in a different way, we must be the first to change.

― Nikoleta: I don’t have a problem, I’m a new Tinddaya, and I know what it’s like to feel rejected -she shrugs-, I’ll be with her.

― Lázaro: I’m fine with increasing the protection of As Talladas, I’ll do everything in my power -he shrugs and stretches out the fingers of his bionic hand with a faint smile.

― Uriel: If it eases you all, we can establish a mental link with the little girl, any thoughts against us will be known immediately. I don’t think the child will pose a real threat in the short or medium term if you give us permission to listen to her.

― Koiki: -she runs her hand through her hair and kneads her face- The girl must never know.

― Nél: I agree. She won’t know anything and will be raised in the Mine.

― Rol: Then we agree. As TALLADAS increases development in defence and the child is raised among us under the watchful eye of the Trisib -all present nod.

― Cornelia: The child cannot know she is a necromancer, there will be questions when she grows up, we cannot train her in her own discipline. It is better that she believes she is unique, but that would start with changing the name of her lineage.

― Jimena: She’ll be a lunar alchemist -Cornelia turns to her and looks at her deeply.

― Cor: Which will explain his skills in the dark. It’s perfect -she smiles shyly. Little by little it will be integrated.

― Nél: Very good, it’s simple, but effective. Nobody has seen alchemists, nobody knows what their skills are. I hope Alejo will eventually understand that it’s the best option for everyone.

― Rol: Deer, talk to Spark, Nélida and I will go with you to talk to her parents.

― Dee: Aye, of course.

Spark is sitting at the school’s doorway, his whole back trunk slumped over the seat, his head tucked between his shoulders, kicking at a painting of an elephant, tearing at the wall. Deer is inside, waiting in the hall, biting his nails. Nélida and Roland are talking to the principal and the headmistress.

― Deer: -meets Spark- What are they saying to bring them round?

― Spark: Nelida’s playing one of her music boxes; they’ll have reached the girl’s parents by now.

About ten minutes later a woman appears on the street with the girl and she recognises the two redheads. She goes into the hall, the girl stays with the caretaker and the woman goes into the headmaster’s office. Deer looks at the girl from the doorway.

― Girl: I want to go outside.

― Concierge: I can’t let you out, sweetheart. If you want can leave the concierge’s office, but stay inside the hall -the girl nods. She approaches the door from the inside and knocks on it. Deer approaches and watches her from the other side of the glass.

― Girl: What are you doing here? You’re going to steal the school decorations.

― Dee: No.

― Dee: HA! -he cuts off the laughter by covering his mouth-. That’s good, so it is - Spark pulls down his sunglasses and pulls his sweatshirt up to his nose-. What’s your name, wee villain?

― Girl: Ok, Deer sounds good.

― Dee: Speak English, my lass?

― Girl: Just a bit -smiles.

― Girl: My mum calls me Lux.

― Dee: Well, pleased to meet you, Lux (light) -she finishes her sentence when Nélida and Roland appear, accompanied by the girl’s mother and the management team. They say goodbye, Nélida hands over a card with her contact. They go outside.

― Rol: Come on, guys. We’re done -they walk down the street, heading for a doorway on which to place the doorknob.

― Spa: Have you got it?

― Nél: It depends on his mother. But I have the impression that she will accept.

― Dee: Have you used the music boxes on them?

― Nél: Not with the mother, she is fully aware that her daughter is not like the others and will consider the option of joining us. The other two will not remember what happened when they wake up.

― Dee: Grand.

― Rol: -they arrive at a door- Do you want to stay here, and if the mother calls, we’ll let you know?

― Spa: Aye.

― Dee: We’re staying. Slàn leibh (farewell).

It is 23:42 at night when the phone on the bedside table vibrates. Nélida wakes up and picks it up.

― Nél: Good evening, Carmen. Good to hear from you.

― Carmen: I’ve been thinking a lot about what you said. I know that my girl is special, and right now I can’t give her any more, I barely have enough to keep her fed. And many afternoons she stays alone being so little so that I can clean more houses… If it’s true that you’re going to help us, we’ll move in tomorrow.

― Nél: It will be a real pleasure to have you and Lucía with us. Tomorrow at 12 o’clock in the morning, our colleagues will be there, the same ones who were with us this afternoon. They will bring you to the development centre. I imagine you will have time to pick up.

― Carmen: Don’t worry, there’s not much, just a few suitcases, everything in the house came with the rent. I’ll talk to the owner and we’ll leave the house. 12 o’clock is fine. We will be at the door waiting for them. Thank you very much from the bottom of our hearts. Good night.
